0.289 2026-07-3x
----------------


MAME Testers bugs fixed
-----------------------
- 03342: [Gameplay] (gaelco/gaelco2.cpp) radikalb, surfplnt40, surfplnt: Some graphics are misaligned. (Jos van Mourik)
- 05547: [Crash/Freeze] (pc/ct486.cpp) ct486: The system may fail to boot from MS-DOS 6.22 installation media. (Dirk Best)
- 06026: [Documentation] (sega/model1.cpp) swa: The game title did not include the word “Arcade” at release. (JimCarlTay)
- 06584: [Documentation] sony/zn.cpp) raystorm and clones: The game title is usually written without a space. (JimCarlTay)
- 06586: [Documentation] (namco/namcos23.cpp) gunwars, raceon and clones: Lack of camera emulation is not flagged. (JimCarlTay)
- 06600: [Crash/Freeze] (sega/model1.cpp) netmerc: The emulator may crash due to memory corruption. (Francisco García)
- 07563: [Gameplay] (sega/model1.cpp) wingwar, wingwarj, wingwaru, wingwar360: Bullet collisions don’t register. (Francisco García)
- 07699: [Gameplay] (sega/model1.cpp) swa, swaj: The radar is not displayed correctly. (Francisco García)
- 08584: [Media Support] (commodore/c64.cpp) c64 all versions: Formatting floppy disks is very slow. (Curt Coder)
- 09202: [Sound] (sega/model2.cpp) srallyc and clones: Incorrect sounds are played in attract mode. (Angelo Salese)
- 09345: [Misc.] (nec/pc9821.cpp) pc9821ap2 [Flame Zapper Kotsujin]: Fails to start with an allocation error. (Angelo Salese)
- 09456: [Graphics] (brother/lw840.cpp) lw840: The system does not appear to boot. (Leopold)
- 09462: [Documentation] (sega/model1.cpp) swa, swaj: The release year is incorrect. (JimCarlTay)
- 09463: [Documentation] (seibu/deadang.cpp) popnrun, popnruna: The manufacturer is incorrect. (J.J.Boy)
- 09466: [Graphics] (namco/namcos22.cpp) alpinerd and clones: Distant ground polygons are incorrectly drawn in red. (hap)
- 09467: [Cheat System] Cheat output text is not displayed. (Vas Crabb)
- 09477: [Media Support] (trs/dgnalpha.cpp) dgnalpha: The system will not boot to BASIC or from a floppy disk. (AJR)
- 09478: [Media Support] (wang/wangpc.cpp) wangpc: The system will not boot from floppy disks. (AJR)
- 09479: [Media Support] (ampro/lb186.cpp) lb186: The system will not boot from floppy disks. (AJR)
- 09480: [Documentation] Taito Corporation and its subsidiaries are listed inconsistently for different games. (JimCarlTay)
- 09485: [Crash/Freeze] (kaneko/suprnova.cpp) vblokbrk and clones: Freezes when clearing a stage in final seconds. (Angelo Salese)
- 09492: [DIP/Input] [plugin]: The port name plugin causes a fatal error on start when enabled. (Vas Crabb)

14692: minitel2: Implement sound output for DTMF tones and beeps [fabio-d]
In the minitel2, sound is generated by a speaker connected to the TS7514 modem chip. A subset of its features are implemented in
  this commit:
 - Mode selection, using MCBC and DTMF lines.
 - Shift register for input control data, using the RTS (clock) and PRD (data) lines.
 - Modem register RDTMF, to select what pair of frequencies to generate while in DTMF mode.
 - Modem register RWLO, selecting what to play on the speaker. In particular, monitoring of the transmitted DTMF tones and beep
  generation have been implemented. Monitoring of TX/RX data tones and volume control has NOT been implemented.
 - Modem register RPTF, selecting a filter on the transmitted frequencies. Only the filter on DTMF tones (to select only one of the
  two frequencies in the current pair) has been implemented.
This subset of features is enough to play all the sounds generated by the "demov1" ROM and the original ROM (beep on invalid
  keypress, DTMF dialing tones).
Note that, in order to hear DTMF tones in the original ROM, the "FV" (_fréquence vocale_) option must be selected in the
  configuration screen:
<img width="668" height="556" alt="DC_FV" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/b0cb8727-7099-4a6e-8c31-f1f6736efba4" />
This commit also simplifies the pre-existing logic to generate a carrier-detect signal when the line control relay is closed.
cc @jfdelnero 

14947: Add support for Atmel ATV750 [inaxeon]
This PR is to add support to jedutil for the Atmel ATV750 which is a drop in replacement for the 22V10 type but offering double the
  number of registers and pterms.

15365: cpu/m68000: Update bftst to use reluctant reads when fetching data from memory [briceonk]
When working on the NWS-1250 (#15364), I was seeing occasional segmentation faults (more easily reproduced in the 12MB RAM
  configuration and in the NEWS-OS 4.1C install process). I have traced out the results of my debug below, but I am not a 68k
  expert, so I would appreciate a second set of eyes on this! I might have missed something.
First, the offending kernel stack trace:
<img width="1739" height="657" alt="mem_bus_error"
  src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/914c7bc4-8a59-457c-bd9f-c6918d587461" />
The instruction causing a bus error is 0x8005dda8.
Instruction: BFTST (A5) {30:2}; (2+)
A5 = 0x80a0dffc bit 30 is at byte address 0x80a0dfff, and the instruction is testing the last two bits in the page because of the
  following walk:
TC = 0x80c019a0 (0b10000000110000000001100110100000)
  E -> 1 (translation enabled)
  U -> 0b00000 (unused)
SRE -> 0 (supervisor root pointer disabled)
FCL -> 0 (function code lookup disabled, first table level indexed by logic address field defined by TIA)
 PS -> 0b1100 (4KiB)
 IS -> 0b0000 (no shift)
TIA -> 0b0001 (1 bit for top level)
TIB -> 0b1001 (9 bits for second level)
TIC -> 0b1010 (10 bits for third level)
TID -> 0b0000 (stops before fourth level)
A5 = 0x80a0dffc
0x80a0dffc -> top level bit = 1, crp + 8 * 1 = 0xa2800 + 8 * 1 = 0xa2808 has the level 1 descriptor
0xc00a2808 = 0x01ff010a 0x000a2000 -> second level (9) bits = 0x2, 0xa2000 + 4 * 2 = 0xa2008 has the level 2 descriptor
0xc00a2008 = 0x0009600a -> third level (10) bits are 0x20d, 0x96000 + 0x20d * 4 = 0x96834 (PTE)
0xc0096834 = 0x00bbd019
Results of the table walk:
<img width="1042" height="1321" alt="mem_walk" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/82ceec2b-ba77-48cc-b5d1-697b04b31962"
  />
However, the next PTE is 0. Therefore, when the `bftst` instruction calls m68ki_read_32(0x80a0dfff), it hits a bus error after the
  first byte.
Based on how some others like `bfext` are implemented (like in `xebd0_bfexts_l_ai_234fc`, which is an example of one that uses the
  change applied to `bftst` here), it seems like this is a bug. However, I see some of the other bitflag instructions are
  implemented like `bftst`, so I am not 100% sure.

15369: dsp563xx/hi08: forward DSP HTX writes to the host [felipesanches]
<img width="1920" height="1080" alt="image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/769130ee-1095-472b-b78c-0cd45183b787" />
`void hi08_device::htx_w(u32 data)` only logged the outgoing word; it did not store it in `m_htx` or update `m_isr`. Host-side reads
  of HRX (offsets 5/6/7) therefore always returned zero and the host never saw ISR_RXDF asserted, so any 80C535 firmware polling HSR
  for a reply from the DSP after the bootstrap upload would spin forever.
Store the word the DSP placed on HTX into m_htx, raise ISR_RXDF (which in turn asserts host-side HRDF and clears DSP-side HTDE
  through hsr_r's view), and let the host's read of the trigger byte at offset 7 clear ISR_RXDF so the DSP can transmit the next
  word.
With this in place virusb boots all the way to its interactive UI: the LCD shows the patch / parameter pages and the panel buttons
  drive the menu.

15370: taito/taito_l.cpp: New WORKING clone [sergiopolog]
Play Girls 2 (Europe) [Kebrank]
New working clone found in a PCB with: "PG2_1E" on the program EPROM label. Current parent set has it as:  "pg2_1j" and shows a
  Japan warning screen when booting up, so I presume this new set is for Europe (E). The warning screen is not present in this new
  set.
The only difference between them are several bytes always changed from "00" to "01" on many places, defining that "00" would be the
  value for Japan version and "01" for the Europe version.
I put this one as parent set and moved the current existing one as a clone: `plgirls2j`
I also noticed that the dumped bootleg has more in common with this new Europe set than the Japan set.
Find pictures of the actual PCB and EPROM label below:
https://i.imgur.com/3xfyxkI.jpeg
https://i.imgur.com/4vZYaTI.jpeg

15371: add Casiotone RC-1 driver, fix uPD7801 INT0 behavior [devinacker]

15372: toshiba/t250.cpp: New driver for Toshiba T-200 and T-250 [davidlrand]
8085-based CP/M business machines from 1981.  Both share the same main board; only the floppy subsystem differs.  T-200 uses 5.25"
  DSDD floppies, T-250 uses 8" DSDD.
Verified working subsystems:
- Intel 8085A at 16 MHz / 3 (5.333 MHz)
- 64 KB RAM with boot ROM bank-switched at 0x0000-0x0FFF
- HD6845 CRTC with 10-pixel wide character cell and VPG-RAM glyph storage (CPU shares VPG with the 6845 via 0xF800 bank-switch)
- Hardware inverse video (XOR over the full 10x10 cell)
- Intel 8279 keyboard + keypad (matrix + rl8 path, shift/ctrl mods)
- 2 kHz keyboard buzzer (BUZZ bit of 0xC0 bank latch)
- Intel 8257 DMA, including ch0/ch1 memory-to-memory used by the BIOS clear-screen and scroll routines
- NEC uPD765A FDC with external RESET latch at port 0xF0 bit 7 (T-250: 8 MHz, 500 kbps MFM; T-200: 4 MHz, 250 kbps, motor enable at
  port 0xF0 bit 6)
- Centronics parallel printer
- Intel 8253 PIT counter 2 generating baud-rate clock (1.9968 MHz input per the actual CCM board, NOT the standard 1.8432 crystal)
- Intel 8251 USART (full TX + RX path verified end-to-end)
- Optional SASI hard-disk interface (8155 host adapter to nscsi_bus; empty by default, attach via -sasi:0 harddisk -hard1 disk.chd)
CP/M 2.20 / 2.32 boots cleanly from period-original floppies on both variants.  RST 6.5 interrupt source dispatched via the
  read-only register at port 0xD0 (rdint).
Driver and both ROM dumps contributed by the original BIOS author. Hardware-archive repository at:
  https://github.com/davidlrand/toshiba-t250-t200

15382: adp/stella8085.cpp: Fix 4040 board IO [stonedDiscord]
This maps the correct IO addresses for the 4040 boards, making bahia,disc,dpplstrt and discoly interactable.
This also clears up what IO 0x73 is, it's the 8255 control register.
The 8255 was replaced with discrete ICs and a fixed direction during the transition to 4087 but software on there will still set up
  the control registers.
This is best visible on discoly, before it would do nothing.
With this change it will blink the fault lamp and after pressing Up 1,- + Initialize it will show a fault code on the service
  display.

15385: formats/imd_dsk: Add save support (IMD write for flopconvert) [davidlrand]
## Summary
IMD has been read-only in MAME's flopimg pipeline since 2017 — `imd_format::load()` parses ImageDisk files, but flux-level images
  (MFI, fresh Greaseweazle captures, running emulation state) cannot round-trip back to IMD. This change implements
  `imd_format::save()` so that `flopconvert mfi imd ...` works directly, and any driver-side "save as IMD" path becomes available.
After this:
```
floptool help formats
...
  imd     rw - IMD disk image [imd]
...
```
## Approach
Per-track decode-by-scoring rather than driver-supplied geometry:
- New `imd_format::extract_track_rich()` walks the MFM/FM bitstream once and produces records carrying **physical sector order**,
  **DAM-deleted flag**, and **CRC validity for both address and data records**. The shared `extract_sectors_from_bitstream_*_pc()`
  helpers in `flopimg.cpp` discard this info; rather than widen those helpers and disturb every caller, this format keeps a local
  extractor.
- New `imd_format::detect_track()` probes four common `(encoding, cell_size)` combinations per track — MFM at 1000 / 2000 ns and FM
  at 2000 / 4000 ns, covering 250 and 500 kbps in both modes — and picks the one with the most cleanly-decoded sectors (scored by
  IDAM CRC). Re-runs per track so **mixed-density disks decode honestly** without per-driver tuning (e.g. CP/M boot disks with an
  FM-128B boot track on cyl 0 head 0 and MFM data tracks elsewhere).
- New `imd_format::save()` walks tracks in IMD's canonical order, emits per-track records with the right mode byte, sector-numbering
  map, optional cyl/head maps, and per-sector type byte (with the compressed-fill optimisation for all-same-byte sectors).
  Deleted-DAM and bad-CRC sectors round-trip to IMD types 3-8 as appropriate.
- Header timestamp + comment are **preserved across an IMD -> IMD pass** via a `mutable` string member captured on `load()`; other
  source formats produce a fresh `IMD 1.20: <localtime>` header with a short `Created by MAME flopconvert` annotation.
Flux-noise phantom IDAMs (random sync hits in marginal flux) are filtered two ways:
1. **Push gate**: at the extractor's push sites, sectors with both IDAM and DAM CRCs bad are dropped — noise rarely produces a
  CRC-valid coincidence on either side.
2. **Dedup pass**: after extraction, candidates sharing a sector ID are deduplicated by a trust ranking that prefers has_data + good
  CRCs over no data over bad CRC.
Both filters were needed — the second one catches the case where a phantom IDAM has a unique fake sector ID and a fake idam_size
  that would otherwise trip the mixed-sector-sizes guard.
## Validation
- **24 / 24** disks in a local 5.25" T-200 archive (mixed-density boot tracks; bad-CRC sectors in unused CP/M space; rescue-merged
  tracks combined from multiple Greaseweazle captures) round-trip **byte-identical at the sector-data level** through `flopconvert
  imd -> mfi -> imd`. (Header timestamps differ as expected.)
- Cross-validated against a Greaseweazle-produced IMD of an 8" T-250 boot disk: byte-identical sectors after `imd -> mfi -> imd`.
- Per-track encoding detection produces the expected mode byte: e.g. for a Toshiba T-200 boot disk, cyl 0 head 0 -> mode 02 (FM 250)
  / 128 B, rest -> mode 05 (MFM 250) / 256 B.
## Limitations
These cases **refuse the save with a clear error** rather than producing corrupt output:
- Tracks with mixed sector sizes — IMD has no way to represent that.
- Tracks at 300 kbps (IMD modes 1 / 4) fall back to mode 5 (MFM 250). Adequate for all CP/M and PC media; rare exotic disks would
  need the probe list extended.
## Test plan
- [x] `flopconvert imd mfi <disk.imd> <disk.mfi>` then `flopconvert mfi imd <disk.mfi> <out.imd>` — verify sector data identical
  across a representative set of clean MFM/FM disks
- [x] Mixed-density disk: per-track encoding round-trips honestly
- [x] Disk with bad-CRC sectors (IMD types 5-8) round-trips with the bad-CRC marking preserved
- [x] Disk with compressed (all-fill) sectors round-trips with the compression preserved
- [x] CI: gcc + clang on Linux, gcc + clang on Windows, clang on macOS (will be exercised when the PR builds)

15387: apple2: improve accelerator delay emulation [arekkusu42]
(followup #15313, #14397)
This PR corrects the emulation of temporary accelerator delays to match observed Zip Chip and ZipGS hardware behavior, and Laser
  128EX documentation.
There are various delay differences in hardware behavior, summarizing here:
* ZipGS and Zip both do software-configurable slot delay on DEVSEL C0nx, but not IOSEL Cnxx or C800 ROM 
* Laser does hardware-configurable slot delay on some DEVSEL C0nx, IOSEL Cnxx, and C800 ROM
* ZipGS optional speaker delay on C030, Zip on C03x, Laser no delay
* ZipGS optional paddle delay on C020/70, Zip only on C070, Laser no delay
* ZipGS-only optional video counter delay on C02E/7E, optional IRQ delay
* ZipGS cancels any current delay on a write to any Zip register, Zip Chip only cancels when bit 7 of C05E is set (and this
  continues to disable all future delays until unset.)
All of that matches the existing (apocryphal) documentation, except for the C03x speaker part which is not surprising, just
  undocumented.
The IIgs also has undocumented slot delay interaction with the C036 motor-on detection bits: in addition to slowing to 1MHz when the
  motor is actually on, the slot 4-7 bits override reads of the Zip's C05C slot register.  This is indirectly user-observable by
  toggling CDA > Slots > Slot 6 (and rebooting): the "Disk Port" default will cause the firmware to detect Disk ] firmware in slot 6
  and set the corresponding C036 motor-on bit during boot, which causes the Zip to temporarily delay on any C0Ex access (even when
  C05C or [the hardware DIP switch is set to force slot 6 "fast".)
I verified these changes against a 15MHz/64k ZipGSX in an Apple IIgs ROM3.
Thanks to David Schmidt @david-schmidt and Tom Greene @txgx42 for //c+ and Zip Chip-8 hardware testing.
<br>
Code comments:<details>
* apple2gs simply deletes most `accel_slot()` uses, athough `c080_w()` adds it (testing of this must carefully avoid false-read
  cycles in i.e. STA,Y)
* apple2e removes some `accel_slot()` uses, and `laser_slot()` wraps Laser-only cases 
* unprotected `address_map` handlers are wrapped in lambdas
* `accel_temp_delay()` and `accel_stop_delay()` helpers simply reduce copy-paste (and bottleneck instrumentation)
The Laser 128 family has unemulated hardware switches to toggle INT/EXT PORT 5 and PORT 7 which interact with the acceleration.
  Currently laser128, laser128o and las128ex have slots 5 and 7 hardcoded to accept external `a2bus` devices (so the slot 5 ramdisk
  via `-ramsize` can't work), while las128e2 has them hardcoded to internal (so i.e. `-sl7 cffa2` can't work.)  This PR doesn't
  attempt to address that, but for now, I have hooked up `m_accel_slotspk` to slow down slots 5 and 7 only when devices are in those
  slots.
</details>
<br>
User-visible changes:<details>
The most noticeable change is that apple2e/c machines configured with a Zip Chip now boot with a high-pitched beep.  This matches
  hardware behavior audible here.  (apple2cp firmware overwrites the Zip registers during boot so the beep is slow, but slightly
  off-pitch for other reasons.)
Zip Chip Utilities are unchanged (System Check passes, Cache Diagnostics fail.)
The ZipGS 1.2 CDA and FTA Zip CDA are slightly improved: they now show the Slot and Misc settings match the expected defaults.  Andy
  McFadden's Zippy) is now usable.
This won't affect users with the default Zip settings, but if slot 3 is programmatically set to "slow" then the PR#3 80-column
  firmware now remains fast like hardware.  Before this PR it was noticeably slow due to the incorrect IOSEL delays.
Implementing the IIgs counter delay fixes some tearing visible in FTA Modulae, "Fill Mode Three-Dee":
<img width="704" height="462" alt="MODULAE_tearing_before"
  src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/3d819ce3-d98b-4d84-9a5f-db92c28c08b8" />
</details>
<br>
Testing:<details>
Total Replay's Zip (and Laser) acceleration continues to work, speeding up for decompression and disabling for play (or relying on
  CPS Follow on the IIgs.)
	
Light printer testing of `-sl1 grapplus -sl1:grapplus:prn ap2000`, `-sl1 parallel -sl1:parallel:prn ap2000`, `-parallel ap2000`) via
  PR#1 or Combined Enhanced Graphics continues to work.
Light serial testing of `-modem null_modem -bitbanger modem.txt` (configuring Laser Ctrl-P-Reset > Serial and Machine Configuration
  > RS-232 to matching 7N1 9600 or 19200 baud) via PR#2 continues to work.  las128ex and las128e2 (toggling speeds with
  Ctrl-1/2/3-Reset) now slow down serial access to 1 MHz per the documentation; listing a large BASIC program over serial is
  noticeably slower.
On apple2cp, the serial port continues to work after configuring manually:
```

Ctrl-A 1D
Ctrl-A 15B
Ctrl-A L
```
My previous WIP unit tests are updated here:

15411: bus/nscsi/s1410: Add an optional cylinder-aware seek-timing model [davidlrand]
## Summary
Adds an optional, cylinder-aware seek-timing model to the Xebec S1410
SASI Winchester controller, mirroring the approach taken for the
DTC-510 in #15406.  No call from a driver -> behavior is **unchanged**
for the existing s1410 users (victor9k, mikromik, x37_sasi, db4105,
idpartner_sasi); they continue to see the legacy flat 85 ms
per-command delay.
## API
```c++
void set_seek_timing(uint32_t track_us,
                     uint32_t average_us,
                     uint32_t full_us,
                     uint32_t rpm,
                     uint8_t  interleave);
```
The first three arguments are the drive's published seek times in
microseconds; `rpm` is spindle speed; `interleave` is the sector
interleave of the format.  Called once after the controller is
attached at a slot, typically from `machine_start()`.
## Model
`scsi_data_command_delay()` is replaced for `READ` / `WRITE` / `SEEK`
with:
- **same cylinder** (`dist == 0`): only `rev_us * interleave / spt`,
  so sequential I/O on one cylinder pays only the rotational gap.
- **cylinder change**: `rev_us / 2` (half-revolution rotational
  latency) + `track_us` + `range_us * (dist / ncyl)^exp`.
`range_us = full_us - track_us`, and `exp` is solved from the three
calibration points so an average-length seek (~1/3 stroke) costs
exactly `average_us`.  For the ST-506/ST-412 (track 3 ms, avg 85 ms,
full 205 ms) this gives `exp ~= 0.82`, which captures the
controller's "burst mode" multi-cylinder seek: faster than naive
3 ms/cyl stepping for long seeks while remaining cheap for short
ones.
Head position is tracked across commands (`m_last_cylinder`), so
sustained sequential reads on one cylinder are no longer billed an
average seek per sector -- the dominant cost the old flat 85 ms
delay imposed.
## ST-506 and ST-412 examples
The header documents both invocations side by side.  They use
identical parameters because the controller's burst mode adapts to
the CHD's reported cylinder count via `image->get_info().cylinders`;
the 5 MB ST-506 (153 cyl) and 10 MB ST-412 (306 cyl) are
distinguished only by physical media size, not by the timing call:
```c++
// Seagate ST-506 (5 MB, 153 cyl):
if (auto *s = dynamic_cast<nscsi_s1410_device *>(subdevice("sasi:0:s1410")))
    s->set_seek_timing(3000, 85000, 205000, 3600, 1);
// Seagate ST-412 (10 MB, 306 cyl) -- identical parameters:
if (auto *s = dynamic_cast<nscsi_s1410_device *>(subdevice("sasi:0:s1410")))
    s->set_seek_timing(3000, 85000, 205000, 3600, 1);
```
Timing per the **ST412 OEM Manual (April 1982)**: 3600 RPM,
track-to-track 3 ms, average 85 ms, full-stroke (max) 205 ms with
"fast seek / burst mode" (settling already included), 32 sec/track,
256 B/sector.  Sector interleave is the host CBIOS's responsibility
-- 1:1 for the Big Board II's Cal-Tex monitor.
## Compatibility
No existing in-tree driver calls `set_seek_timing()`, so behavior for
all current s1410 users is identical: `m_seek_model` stays `false`
and the legacy `return attotime::from_msec(85)` branch is taken
unchanged.  New drivers (e.g. a Big Board II 8" SASI workstation;
work in progress out-of-tree) opt in by adding one call from
`machine_start()`.
## Validation
With a Big Board II driver calling
`set_seek_timing(3000, 85000, 205000, 3600, 1)` against a 153-cyl
ST-506 CHD, observed times match the user's first-hand experience
with the real hardware -- both the snappiness of sustained streaming
on one cylinder and the painfully-slow head movement of long seeks
("painfully slow stepper design", per the report that originally
motivated this change).
## Test plan
- [ ] `make SUBTARGET=tiny SOURCES=src/mame/xerox/bigbord2.cpp` builds
- [ ] `mame -listdevices victor9k | grep -i s1410` unchanged
- [ ] No call to `set_seek_timing()` -> `mame victor9k` floppy/HD
      behavior identical to master
- [ ] Boot a driver that opts in -> sustained read throughput on a
      single cylinder no longer pays an 85 ms-per-sector penalty;
      long random seeks bill ~205 ms

15412: xerox/bigbord2: Boot CP/M, wire SASI HD, mark working [davidlrand]
## Summary
Brings the Big Board II driver up from `MACHINE_NOT_WORKING`. Two
commits:
1. **xerox/bigbord2: Boot CP/M, wire SASI hard disk, fix warm-boot DRQ race**
2. **xerox/bigbord2: Mark working**
Five inter-related changes, all local to `bigbord2.cpp`:
### 1. 74LS151 DMA-RDY mux: track the selected input dynamically
`syslatch2` bits 0-2 select which of eight RDY sources feeds the
Z80 DMA's `/RDY` pin; the existing driver only re-evaluated the mux
inside `syslatch2_w`, so per-byte FDC DRQ / SIO WRDY edges that
happen *after* a `syslatch2` write never propagated to the DMA --
the boot DMA read of the CP/M loader set WD179x bit 2 (Lost Data)
and the BIOS printed `"BIOS error on A: drq"`.
Fix: store `m_dma_sel` + an `m_cc[8]` cache of all eight mux inputs,
then have `fdc_drq_w` / `sio_wrdya_w` / `sio_wrdyb_w` (and
`sasi_req_w` below) call `m_dma->rdy_w()` whenever their index
matches `m_dma_sel`.
### 2. Soft-reset re-init
The state above (`m_dma_sel`, `m_cc[]`, DMA RDY level, `m_fdc_rq`,
the NMI line) was left stale across a soft reset; cold restarts
worked, soft restarts behaved non-deterministically. `machine_reset()`
now restores it to power-on values.
### 3. FDC INTRQ + DRQ -> Z80 `/NMI` gate (HALT-conditional)
Per the schematic, the WD179x's INTRQ (pin 39) and DRQ are OR'd,
inverted and AND'd with `/HALT` to drive `/NMI` -- so the CBIOS's
HALT+INI PIO path moves one byte per NMI while the CPU is halted,
but the gate stays quiet during a DMA transfer (CPU bus-released,
not halted). Implemented via the Z80 `halt_cb()` callback (zero
polling latency) + a single `m_fdc_rq` byte tracking INTRQ in bit 0,
DRQ in bit 1, and NMI-asserted in bit 7. Needed for CBIOS variants
that use the HALT+INI path on warm boot; the stock V1.1 monitor in
`bigbrdii.u85` is interrupt-driven and never halts, so the gate
correctly stays quiet for it.
### 4. Race-window mask in `fdc_status_r`
MAME's `wd_fdc::command_end()` clears BUSY without dropping DRQ when
LOST_DATA isn't set -- the code has a literal `// TBD: lost data
should probably negate DRQ` comment. That exposes a transient
`BUSY=0 + DRQ=1` window real WD179x DMA hardware doesn't expose
(arbitration grabs the trailing byte before BUSY drops). Without
this mask the V1.1 CBIOS's `DISKOP` polling exits its "wait for
BUSY=0" loop, masks the returned status with `AND $9F`, sees bit 1
(DRQ) set, and reports `"BIOS error on A: drq track N sector M"`
on every warm boot. Re-assert BUSY in the status read while the
race window is open so the BIOS keeps polling; the DMA drains the
data register within microseconds and the next read returns a
clean `STS=00`. The byte stays in the FDC's data register -- the
mask only delays when the BIOS sees the command complete, it
doesn't fabricate or hide data.
### 5. Xebec S1410 SASI hard disk subsystem
Adds a small in-driver `bigbord2_sasi_host_device` that bridges
between the BB-II's two SASI ports (D8 data / D9 status) and the
nscsi bus + s1410 target. D9 status bits map BSY=0x02, MSG=0x08,
C/D=0x20, I/O=0x01 and (for the DMA-variant ST412 BIOS) REQ=0x80.
SEL and RST come in through `outlatch1` (U96, an LS259 addressable
latch) Q3 and Q2. SASI REQ is surfaced via `req_cb()` so it can
feed mux input 7 to the DMA RDY; the cbios.s2 INIR variant uses the
DMA's read-status command to wait for REQ, the DMA-variant reads
D9 bit 7 directly.
An optional `set_seek_timing()` call from `machine_start()` opts
the s1410 into a realistic ST-506 cylinder-aware seek model
(3 ms / 85 ms / 205 ms at 3600 RPM, 1:1 interleave -- per the
April 1982 ST412 OEM manual).
PAL ROM declarations for U23 (PAL16L8) and U34 (PAL10L8) are added
as `NO_DUMP` -- the parts have their security fuse blown, but the
mem/io decode is already hard-coded from the CUPL equations in
`roms/bigbord2/BB2_U23_Replacement.txt` and its U34 counterpart,
so the ROMs are cosmetic for the audit.
## Dependency
The s1410 `set_seek_timing()` call depends on **PR #15411**
(`bus/nscsi/s1410: Add an optional cylinder-aware seek-timing
model`). #15411 is itself self-contained and merges cleanly on
master in isolation; this PR sits on top of it.
## What has been tested
- **Stock CBIOS, V1.1 9-March-83 monitor** in `roms/bigbord2/bigbrdii.u85`
  (already in the MAME tree; signature string visible in the ROM):
  boots CP/M 2.2 cleanly off the standard `DDBOOT.S1` 8" SSDD floppy
  (FM 26x128 boot track 0 + MFM 8x1024 data tracks 1-76). Sustained
  warm-boot CCP reloads, file copy A: -> B: and back, full directory
  listings, EDitor sessions, all without the spurious `"drq"` error
  the warm-boot DRQ race used to trigger.
- **bb2mon (24-JUNE-82) and BBII_Monitor_V6.12 (2-FEB-85) CBIOS
  source variants**, cross-referenced from the bitsavers Big Board
  II archive at <https://bitsavers.org/bits/Ferguson/Big_Board_II/>
  (`bb2mon/DISKIO.MAC`, `BBII_Monitor_V6.12/DISKIO.MAC`,
  `BBII_CBIOS/files/deblock.mac` -- the latter has the canonical
  bit-to-error-string table at line 819, where bit 1 = `"drq"` and
  bit 2 = `"overrun"`). All three variants read the same way as the
  V1.1 monitor in `bigbrdii.u85`: BUSY-polling `DISKOP`, `AND $9F`
  mask. The fix-window mask in `fdc_status_r` applies uniformly.
- **Seagate ST-506 (5 MB, 153 cyl) via the S1410** using the
  CYLS:306, HEADS:4, SECS:32, BPS:256 CHD geometry the cbios.s2
  INIR variant expects (`HSTTRK*32 + sector` LBA, 256 B/sector per
  the `WINSTUFF` DPB). Phase chain completes cleanly all the way
  through DATA-IN -> STATUS -> MSG-IN -> bus-free; 300 KB
  sustained reads take ~65 s, matching the user's first-hand
  experience with the real hardware.
- The Xebec **ST-412 (10 MB, 306 cyl)** path is structurally
  identical -- same controller, same DMA-mux feed, same
  `set_seek_timing()` parameters -- but has not been live-validated
  on this branch.
- **Sound (beeper), CRT (6845 + char/attr RAM), keyboard
  (including control characters), SIO channel B over `-rs232b`**:
  all reachable from the standard CP/M shell, no observed
  regressions relative to the legacy `bigboard` driver.

15414: formats/mfi_dsk: drop debug histogram dump that segfaults on small MFI [felipesanches]
`mfi_format::load()` ended with a debug block that dumped a per-track cell-length histogram to **stderr**, walking a hard-coded
  46-track x 2-side grid. `get_buffer()` only bounds-checks with **assert** (compiled out in release builds), so on an MFI smaller
  than 46 tracks or single-sided the loop indexes track_array past its end and segfaults.
Remove the debug dump; `load()` now just returns once the tracks are in.

15417: colonial/sb80.cpp: New working system, Colonial Data Services SB-80 [davidlrand]
## Summary
Adds a working driver for the Colonial Data Services Corp. **SB-80** — a 1982-vintage Z80 CP/M 2.2 single-board computer that I
  designed the firmware for, and shipped to OEM customers from April 1984 onward.
The system: Z80A at 4 MHz with 64 KB DRAM (9-bit parity), WD1793 floppy controller driving 8" Shugart 800/850-series drives (DSDD,
  IBM-3740 boot tracks + 8×1024 MFM data tracks), Z80 SIO + Z80 CTC + Z80 PIO peripherals, COM8116-family dual baud-rate generator,
  and a 256-byte bootstrap ROM banked out via `OUT ($EA),A` once the disk-loaded BOOT routine takes over. Headless serial-console
  design — the standard product had no on-board video, no parallel keyboard, a serial terminal hung off SIO channel A.
Boots `CP/M 2.2 V1.22  Dave Rand  04/15/84` end-to-end to the `A0>` prompt with the CTC-cascaded 40 Hz time-of-day interrupt
  running, on real boot disks from my own archive (verified at <https://github.com/mamedev/mame/issues/15416> against the Don Maslin
  1993 archive copy of the same CBIOS).
## ROM provenance
The 256-byte bootstrap ROM at `roms/sb80/bootrom.bin` (CRC32 `FA521576`, SHA1 `32153db6f37e7f5666e89aa0b8712d79daafeb4b`) is
  reconstructed from my contemporaneous source listing `bootrom.prn`, which assembled to the binary that I programmed onto the
  actual production EPROMs in 1984. The chain is: my listing → my own EPROM programmer → production silicon → production hardware.
  The binary in this PR is bit-identical to what the production chips ran, with the lineage going through me at every step.
I still have two of those production boards in my possession (S/N 1692 unmodified, S/N 1693 with a contemporaneous 256K
  bank-switching mod I designed in late 1983 / early 1984); should a maintainer ever want a chip-pull-and-dump cross-check I'm happy
  to do one, but the listing-to-EPROM chain makes that a verification step, not a provenance question.
## Hardware lineage notes
Verified against the BIOS source, the November 1982 *BYTE* magazine review of the SB-80, and direct inspection of both surviving
  boards:
- **Z80A @ 4 MHz**, IM 2, vector table at `$FF00`.
- **WD1793** at ports `$FC-$FF`, drive/density/side select latch at `$ED`. DRQ wired to Z80 `/NMI`; the BIOS NMI handler at `$0066`
  does `EXAF / EXX / INI / EXX / EXAF / RETN` (one byte per dispatch). INTRQ not wired to the CPU — the BIOS polls BUSY in DBUSY.
- **Z80 SIO** ports `$F4-$F7` — data at `$F4`/`$F5`, control at `$F6`/`$F7`. Non-standard ordering (data-first then control),
  neither `ba_cd_*` nor `cd_ba_*` helper matches; each port is mapped individually.
- **Z80 CTC** ports `$F0-$F3`. Channels 0 and 1 unused by the CBIOS in the standard configuration; channel 2 timer mode (×16
  prescaler, divide by 25) → 10 kHz pulse train; channel 3 counter mode (divide by 249) cascaded from channel 2's ZC, yielding the
  40 Hz tick that drives `clock:` in the BIOS.
- **COM8116-family BRG** (SMC COM5016 / COM8116 / WD-1943-00 / AY-5-8116 STD) at ports `$E8`/`$E9`, fed by a 5.0688 MHz crystal.
  Programmable rates 5..F map to 300..19200 baud, matching the BIOS' `B3..B19` equates exactly.
- **Z80 PIO** present on the board (Centronics on channel A via the J1 riser daughtercard, per the *BYTE* review). My v1.22 CBIOS
  stubs `list:`/`listst:`/`prtst:` to plain `ret`, so the printer path isn't exercised — the PIO is instantiated for the daisy chain
  but its channel-A handlers are left floating. Whoever picks up printer support in a follow-up gets it wired easily.
- **Drive READY** — the Shugart 800/850-series 8" drives the SB-80 shipped with run on a 120V AC spindle motor with no host
  motor-control line, so the drive-select latch has no `/MOTOR` bit. `machine_reset()` asserts `mon_w(0)` on every populated drive
  so the WD1793 sees READY for type-I commands; this is period-correct, not a workaround.
What's *not* in this PR (deferred to follow-ups):
- 5.25" drive option (mentioned in the *BYTE* back-cover ad; same WD1793, different geometry).
- Winchester host adapter on the 50-pin expansion bus (Colonial Data's 10-40 MB SASI option).
- Cache-CBIOS variant `sb80c.asm` v2.00 12/24/83 (track-caching, banner `53K CACHE`).
- PIO Centronics path.
- 256K bank-switching mod (S/N 1693 hardware variant).
- Optional video / extended-I/O subblock — designed into the PCB at sockets U46, U48-U50, U57-U66, U67-U75 but never populated by
  Colonial Data on any board in the product lifetime (BYTE 1982 review board, S/N 1692, S/N 1693 all empty in the same positions).
  Documented in the driver header for whoever cares; not modelled.
## Disclosure: in-driver workaround for #15416
The driver includes a small workaround in `fdc_drq_w()` that gates the Z80 NMI line transitions through a 6 µs settle floor. Without
  this, MAME's `wd_fdc::set_drq()` combined with `z80`'s NMI-dispatch timing fires a nested NMI inside my BIOS NMI handler under
  heavy CTC-INT-preemption load, scribbles garbage on BIOS working memory, and CCP load fails. The investigation, including a
  per-EXX trace and my testimony that the production hardware never had this problem, is filed as
  <https://github.com/mamedev/mame/issues/15416> — request for architectural-direction guidance on the upstream fix path.
The workaround is openly disclosed in a block comment above the relevant `fdc_drq_w()` body and the CTC-config section, with a
  forward reference to #15416. It's narrowly scoped (driver-local, no `wd_fdc` changes) and only engages when a DRQ rising edge
  would arrive within 6 µs of the previous falling edge — which under normal byte timing (~25 µs at 500 kbps MFM) is never. Should
  the upstream `wd_fdc` fix in #15416 land, this workaround can be deleted in a follow-up PR with no behaviour change.
## Test plan
- [x] `make SOURCES=src/mame/colonial/sb80.cpp` builds clean against current `upstream/master`.
- [x] `mame -validate sb80` passes silently.
- [x] `mame -verifyroms sb80` returns "romset sb80 is good".
- [x] Cold boot from `sb80-dr.imd` (the Don Maslin 1993 archive copy of my v1.22 CBIOS, IBM-3740 8" DSDD) reaches the `A0>` prompt
  with the period-correct banner.
- [x] Multiple boot disks from my archive mount and access cleanly through CCP.
- [x] Warm-boot CCP reload (`^C` from `A0>`) completes without spurious BIOS errors.
- [x] 10-second runtime stable, CTC 40 Hz BIOS-clock tick running, no `LOST_DATA` from the wd_fdc, no nested-NMI corruption.
## Status
`MACHINE_SUPPORTS_SAVE`. Cold-boot, warm-boot, file access, and sustained runtime all verified working with the period-correct CTC
  cascade and time-of-day clock enabled.

15418: imagedev/floppy: derive twosid_r() from media variant [davidlrand]
Reads media-sided-ness from the floppy_image variant tag instead of `get_actual_geometry()`.
Real drives sense single- vs double-sided physically (8" Shugart TS# on pin 30, derived from index-hole position); the signal comes
  from the media, not from whether the host has happened to write to head 1.
Before: a freshly-mounted unformatted SSSD image (`get_actual_geometry` returns 0 heads) reports TWO-SIDED to the host BIOS, and the
  answer can flip permanently once a host writes spurious data to head 1.
After: SS variants always report single-sided; DS variants always report double-sided; an unformatted SSSD diskette is correctly
  recognised as single-sided.
No call-site changes needed — all existing `twosid_r()` consumers and the uPD765 ST3 plumbing already do the right thing once the
  function returns correctly.
## Test plan
- [x] Builds clean against current `upstream/master`.
- [x] `floptool flopcreate mfi u8sssd xfer.mfi` → mount on a 2-head 8" system → BIOS now sees TWO-SIDED = false; CP/M `FORMAT`
  correctly produces a single-sided diskette.

  and column count come from the CRTC registers the boot ROM programs.

15427: cem3340.cpp, prophet5.cpp: Implemented bandlimited oscillator sync. [m1macrophage]
Even though a lot of logic is required for sync bandlimiting, which complicates the code somewhat, the idea is not that complex at a
  high level. Comments in the code explain all that.
I started by implementing this logic in R. The script is here. This allowed for quick iteration,  testing of edge cases, and
  inspection of spectra.
The changes were then ported to MAME and verified by routing the oscillators directly to the output, and using `-wavwrite`,
  `-record` and `-playback` to inspect spectra across different operating conditions.
It is encouraging that getting things wrong would visibly mess up the spectra (when parameters were chosen to exercise the right
  edge cases). This boosts confidence that things work well.

15430: kaypro/kaypro.cpp: Emulate the WD1002-HD0 hard disk controller on the Kaypro 10 [davidlrand]
## Summary
Adds emulation of the WD1002-HD0 Winchester controller and its 10 MB ST506 drive to the two Kaypro 10 machine configurations, which
  previously had floppy-only support (the driver carried a `// need to add hard drive & controller` placeholder):
- `kaypro10` — 1983, mainboard 81-180, V1.9 ROM
- `kaypro1084` — 1984, mainboard 81-181, V1.9E ROM
Both boards use the same controller at the same I/O ports and differ only in the surrounding I/O map (the '83 board has no Z80 PIO /
  RTC), so the controller wiring is shared through one `add_hdc()` helper.
## Hardware
The WD1002-HD0 is built around the WD1010 task-file controller already emulated in `machine/wd1010.cpp`. On the Kaypro 10 it appears
  at:
- `0x80` — board sector-buffer data port (the host moves a 512-byte sector through it with a single `INIR`/`OTIR` burst)
- `0x81-0x87` — WD1010 task file (error/precomp, sector count, sector number, cylinder low/high, SDH, status/command)
Drive geometry is 306 cylinders × 4 heads × 17 sectors × 512 bytes (~10 MB); CP/M uses 16 of the 17 sectors per track. An image is
  created with:
    chdman createhd -o kaypro10.chd -chs 306,4,17 -ss 512
## Implementation notes
- A 512-byte board buffer (`m_hdc_buf`) sits between the host and the WD1010: the host fills/drains it at `0x80` while the
  controller drains/fills it through the device's data callbacks. Buffer-ready (`brdy_w`) is asserted when a full sector has moved
  and reset at each command write.
- The Kaypro 10 has a single fixed Winchester whose drive-select latch presents it as WD1010 "drive 1"; the SDH write collapses any
  drive-select onto the one configured drive so the controller always finds the disk.
- No new device type is introduced — this reuses the existing `wd1010_device` and `harddisk_image_device`. The HD devices are
  `optional_device`s and the `machine_start` setup is guarded by `m_hdc.found()`, so floppy-only operation is unchanged.
## Provenance
The port map, command set, geometry and buffer/handshake behaviour were derived from period Kaypro 10 CP/M disk-utility and BIOS
  source and verified against the V1.9 (81-188) and V1.9E (81-302) ROMs.
## Testing
- `kaypro10` and `kaypro1084` both boot CP/M from floppy unchanged.
- With a CHD attached (`-hard1`), the period Kaypro 10 hard-disk utilities run correctly: `FORMAT04.COM` (a partial low-level format
  that is largely a surface verify) completes, and `FINDBAD.COM` reads the entire disk surface (a full bad-sector scan) without
  error.
- Floppy-only operation (no `-hard1`) is unaffected.
## Related
`machine/wd1010.cpp` currently ships with `VERBOSE` set to all log categories, so any WD1010 user (including this one) floods
  `error.log` under `-log`. A separate one-line PR resets that to `(0)`.

15431: machine/wd1010: disable verbose logging by default [davidlrand]
`wd1010.cpp` is committed with
    #define VERBOSE  (LOG_CMD | LOG_INT | LOG_SEEK | LOG_REGS | LOG_DATA)
i.e. every log category enabled, so every WD1010 user (`tekigw`, `tandy2k`, `unixpc`, `kaypro`) floods `error.log` with per-command
  / per-register / per-seek traces under `-log`. Reset to `(0)`, matching the convention for committed device code; the individual
  `LOG_*` categories remain defined for anyone who wants to re-enable them while debugging.

15448: bus/nscsi: move the opt-in seek-timing model into nscsi_harddisk_device [davidlrand]
## Summary
The cylinder-aware seek-timing model added to the Xebec S1410 (#15411) and
proposed for the DTC-510 (#15406) was identical copy/paste between the two
controllers. As suggested by @MooglyGuy on #15406, this hoists the model up
into their common base, `nscsi_harddisk_device`, so it is written once and is
available to any hard-disk controller built on it (including a plain
`NSCSI_HARDDISK`) without subclassing.
**Supersedes #15406** (DTC-510), which is closed in favour of this.
## What moves, and what deliberately does not
- **The opt-in model moves up.** `set_seek_timing()`, the model state, the
  fitted-exponent math (`seek_time()`), and a generic `scsi_data_command_delay()`
  now live in `nscsi_harddisk_device`.
- **The legacy flat 85 ms delay deliberately stays down** in `s1410` and
  `dtc510`. It is a per-controller quirk (Curt Coder added it to s1410 for
  abc1600 formatting; dtc510 inherited it for pc9801), not a property of all
  hard disks. Hoisting it would change behaviour for every `NSCSI_HARDDISK`
  user; keeping it local does not.
## Safety: no change for existing users
The base `scsi_data_command_delay()` returns `attotime::zero` (its inherited
default) unless a driver has called `set_seek_timing()`. So:
- Every current `nscsi_harddisk_device` user is byte-for-byte unchanged.
- `s1410` and `dtc510` keep their exact legacy behaviour: with no
  `set_seek_timing()` call they return the flat 85 ms for their own command
  set; the only difference is they now delegate the *model* branch to the base
  instead of carrying a private copy of it.
There is still no in-tree caller; this is groundwork (a Toshiba T-200/T-250
driver in development uses it for the machine's CDC 9410 Finch).
## Model
When configured, a command pays a real seek only when the target cylinder
changes (derived from the LBA and the CHD geometry); consecutive sectors on
one cylinder pay only rotational latency scaled by the format interleave. The
seek curve is fitted from the drive's published track-to-track / average /
full-stroke figures. The base applies this to the generic SCSI READ/WRITE/SEEK
opcodes in **both** their 6-byte (0x08/0x0a/0x0b) and 10-byte
(0x28/0x2a/0x2b) forms; subclasses with their own dispatch call `seek_time()`
for whatever opcodes move the heads.
## Parameter documentation
`set_seek_timing()`'s header comment now documents how to derive each of the
five parameters from an OEM manual, including the convention that the quoted
"average" seek is a ~1/3-stroke seek (which anchors the fitted exponent) and a
fallback when a datasheet supplies only two of the three seek times.
## Testing
- The `bus/nscsi` sources compile clean with no new warnings.
- `s1410` users (victor9k, mikromik, x37_sasi, db4105, idpartner_sasi) and the
  `dtc510` user (pc9801_27) are unchanged — the legacy path is untouched and
  the model is inert without a `set_seek_timing()` call.

15453: bus/scsi/scsihle: deassert REQ between phases; hold BSY in COMMAND [davidlrand]
Two SCSI-HLE bus-semantics fixes.
1. `scsi_change_phase()` deasserts REQ on entry, so the initiator sees a clean REQ low→high edge with the new phase's C/D-I/O lines
  settled. Previously the per-byte REQ-deassert timer was cancelled by the next phase's re-assert, leaving REQ continuously high
  across e.g. COMMAND→DATA-IN, so an initiator that samples the control lines on the REQ edge read the stale previous-phase lines.
2. A selected target asserts BSY on entry to COMMAND, so a fast SEL deassert racing the sel_timer cannot leave BSY low during the
  connection.
Tested without regression on `v1050` (working; S1410 SASI hard disk: FMTWINCH format + COPYSYS + boot and access) and on an
  in-development Xerox 820-II SASI host adapter. Other legacy `scsihle` machines (fmtowns, pcd, bebox, rmnimbus, model3, slicer)
  were not regression-tested locally; both changes are conservative SCSI bus semantics.

15458: hd44780: fix char size selection in 2-line mode [devinacker]
On the HD44780 and clones, the character size bit is only available in single line mode. This fixes CGRAM characters on `s700` and
  `x7000`, which set both bits for some reason.

15461: xerox/xerox820.cpp: Get the Digital Research Computers Big Board booting CP/M [davidlrand]
The original Ferguson **Big Board** (`bigboard`) and its **MK-82** clone were `MACHINE_NOT_WORKING`. The shared `xerox820.cpp`
  driver configured them like the Xerox 820 — 5.25" SA-400 drives at a 1 MHz WD1771 clock with bit-per-drive select — but the Big
  Board uses **8" SSSD drives read by the WD1771 in FM**, selected by a **binary drive-unit number** in the system PIO port. Three
  changes get both booting CP/M:
- **8" drives + clock + format.** `bigboard()` now fits 8" Shugart SA-800 drives at the 8" 2 MHz WD1771 clock and registers the IMD
  floppy format the disks ship in.
- **Binary drive select.** The monitor's `SELECT` writes a binary unit number (drive 0 = `00`) in the low bits of system-PIO port A,
  demuxed to one select line; the shared `kbpio_pa_w` decoded one-bit-per-drive, so unit 0 selected nothing. An `m_drvsel_binary`
  flag (set only for the Big Board) switches the decode; the Xerox 820 path is unchanged.
- **Motor.** 8" Shugart drives spin continuously (no motor-control line), so the motor is forced on at reset and the WD1771 sees
  READY for the boot ROM's RESTORE.
The data-bus inversion and `/HALT`-gated DRQ→NMI byte transfer the boot relies on are already correct in master (`fdc_r ^ 0xff`,
  `update_nmi`).
`mojmikro` ("Moj mikro Slovenija") is a localized Big Board clone — its 2 KB monitor carries the identical Big Board command set and
  0x00–0x1F I/O map — and already uses the Big Board machine config, so the same fixes bring it up too; promoted to working.
A **`bigboard5`** clone is also added for the common field modification that ran the Big Board from 5.25" drives in place of the
  stock 8" — left `MACHINE_NOT_WORKING` as I have no 5.25" media to verify it.
Tested: `bigboard`, `mk82` and `mojmikro` boot "BIG BOARD CP/M 2.2" to `A>` and `DIR` lists the disk (8" SSSD IMD media from the
  Ferguson Big Board reproduction project). `x820` and the other variants are untouched.

Clones promoted to working
--------------------------

Moj mikro Slovenija [Dave Rand]
New clones marked not working

Big Board (5.25" drives) [Dave Rand]

15464: apple2: improve Zip Chip emulation [arekkusu42]
(followup #15387)
This PR implements most of the missing Zip Chip register behavior:
* implement Ctrl-Reset interaction
* improve annunciator side-effects
* fix unlocking to be pedantically correct
* implement C05B LC bit
* apple2e: implement C05E state switches
* apple2gs: implement CPSFollow
<br>
Code comments:<details>
`accel_full_speed()` / `accel_normal_speed()` are consolidated into `*update_speed()`.  The previous short-circuit to "prevent
  unnecessary reschedules" is removed, as the core already does that.
Previously, C05B bit 5 was not implemented due to inconsistent documentation. //c+ and Zip Chip-8 hardware testing (thanks to David
  Schmidt and Tom Greene) shows that the paper manual is wrong (the electronic manual included on the Zip Chip Utilities disk is
  correct.)
The manual describes C05B bit 7 as both "1.0035 milliseconds" and as "a tap on the Apple Phase 0 clock divided by 1024", but the
  latter description is the correct one, since the stretched PH0 clock differs depending on the NTSC/PAL XTAL.  ZIPTEST (included
  below) measures this pulse rate precisely.
The manual's description of the C05E softswitch bits use "ROMRD" and "RAMBNK"; hardware testing (with ZIPSCAN, included below) shows
  that these correspond to C012 RDLCRAM and C011 RDLCBNK2 but with inverted polarity.
</details>
<br>
User-visible changes:<details>
Most of this is user-invisible.  For example, unlocking the Zip already worked (after fixing 5x/Ax keys in #15387) in all real-world
  scenarios.  Now it also works like hardware in unlikely programmer-error scenarios.
On apple2gs, implementing CPSFollow improves the ZipGS CDEV/CDA, FTA SuperZip CDA, and Zippy: now the measured speed shows something
  approximately correct instead of 1MHz.
On apple2e, implementing C05E improves the Zip Chip Utilities diagnostic, which now shows C05E marching through every bit pattern
  (although the cache tests still completely fail.)
Ctrl-Reset now more closely matches hardware behavior (always restoring default register state on a //e or //c configured with Zip
  Chip, and optionally on the IIgs only if C058 was written to.)  The //c+ always overwrites Zip registers during reset, so is
  unchanged.
</details>
<br>
Testing:<details>
My previous Zip tests are updated again here with improved coverage:
ZipTest_260607.zip
After this PR, ZIPTEST now passes all subtests except for the programmable divisor related actual-speed measurements.
A new test ZIPRESET shows how mutating register state interacts with Ctrl-Reset and ZipGS C058 forcing cold-boot.  After this PR, it
  matches hardware behavior.
Total Replay and light printer and serial testing continue to work.
</details>
<br>
TODO:<details>
All of the Zip registers are now implemented _except_ the C05D programmable clock divisor.  I have that prototyped, but there are
  timing interactions which need more testing.  Some thoughts:
* Cache (and the effect of a cache miss) is not emulated, so all of the Zip timing is only approximate.  Emulating cache is low ROI;
  other than the Zip Chip Utilities diagnostic, no existing software should depend on the exact behavior.
* Patent 4794523 (which does not exactly match details of shipping product) describes a single-entry write-through buffer to cover
  the latency of some 6502 stores.  Back-to-back stores (especially to the zero page or at higher clock rates) should stall, but
  without a compelling example depending on this behavior, this is also low ROI.
* On the other hand, reads from I/O addresses must always be uncached, and this does have an easily-noticable effect in loops like
  the RDKEY firmware cursor blinking: without the stall, the cursor blinks wildly too fast.  apple2e needs something like
  `sync_cycle()` in the `c0xx_r()` handlers.  apple2gs actually needs two versions: `slow_cycle()` for 1MHz access, and
  `sync_cycle()` for uncached access, and this is further complicated by memory refresh stalls.  Accurate emulation of FPI stalls
  isn't possible because the g65816 core is not cycle-exact (it doesn't access memory on the correct cycles, or issue false-reads),
  but maybe they can be approximately averaged over time, at least well enough to fix 3200-color mode?
</details>

15465: hash/sony_news.xml: Update NWU-671CA tape name and comments [briceonk]

15467: xerox/xerox820.cpp: replace bad u35 dump in the 820-II v5.0 v018 BIOS [davidlrand]
The `v50v018` BIOS for the Xerox 820-II carried a `BAD_DUMP` of u35
(537p10830, CRC `cc4e1c2b`) and was commented "shows ROM ERROR", because
that bad dump fails the monitor's power-on ROM self-test.
The good dump of the same part is already present in the romset as
`u35.5.0_537p10830.bin` (CRC `278fa75f`) — it is the u35 the `v50` BIOS
uses. This change points the v018 entry at it and drops the `BAD_DUMP`
flag; no new ROM files are introduced.
The good dump is independently corroborated: it is byte-identical to the
assembled u35 binary found on the original Balcones v5.0 ROM-build floppy
(Don Maslin archive), alongside u33/u34/u36 that match the other dumps in
this set.
Tested: `x820ii -bios v50v018` now reaches its sign-on
("Xerox  v 5.00   1983 Xerox Corp (v018)") instead of ROM ERROR.
`-validate` and `-verifyroms x820ii` pass.

15468: akai/x7000.cpp: add playback and sampling [devinacker]

15469: esq5505.cpp and more: Cleanup Ensoniq VFX family layout loading. [cbrunschen]
Since the recent output_finder changes, output names are now resolved relative to the device that creates them. When the layouts are
  loaded by the esqpanel device, but the outputs created by the esqvfd device contained within it, then for the layout to find the
  outputs it must use a qualified name - "vfd:vfd~n~".
But if we have to use qualified names for some things, we might as well use them for more things throughout the keyboard-specific
  layouts (vfx, vfxsd, sd1, sd132): such as for finding ioports that might not be on the device that is loading the layout.
This allows us to move the setting of these layouts from the esqpanel device (from which ioports can be resolved without a qualified
  name when _it_ loads the layouts) to the keyoard class (`esq5505_state')'s member functions that configure each keyboard - since
  from there, the qualified names allow us to find not only outputs for use by the esqvfd device, but also io ports that are on the
  esqpanel device.
This in turn allows us to get rid of passing a "panel type" in to the esqpanel device so that it would set the correct layout - it
  no longer has to do that.
We can also now set a default layout on the esqvfd2x40_vfx device, just as on the other esqvfd devilces, for completeless if nothing
  else.
All in all a lot simpler and cleaner, I think.
Code updated, and layouts regenerated accordingly.

15483: machine/nscsi_hle: allow targets to defer phase transitions [davidlrand]
First of a three-part series: #15484 (SA1403D controller) and #15485 (xerox820 driver) stack on this.
## Summary
An `nscsi_full_device` target currently has to queue its entire phase sequence
(data, status, message) before returning from `scsi_command()`, so any command
involving slow media work has to complete synchronously inside the command
dispatch.
This makes `TARGET_NEXT_CONTROL` hold the connection in its current state when
the control queue is empty (instead of popping an invalid entry), and resumes
stepping when `scsi_data_in()` / `scsi_data_out()` / `scsi_status_complete()`
push new entries (`scsi_resume_deferred()`). A target can now return from
`scsi_command()` with nothing queued, run the medium operation off a timer,
and queue the data/status phases when it finishes.
First user: the Shugart SA1403D (next PR in the series), whose floppy section
runs real flux-level wd_fdc operations that take emulated milliseconds.
## Safety
Targets that queue everything up front never present an empty queue at
`TARGET_NEXT_CONTROL`, so they never reach the new hold path, and
`scsi_resume_deferred()` is a no-op while the state machine has not yet
advanced (calls from inside `scsi_command()` dispatch). No behavior change
for existing nscsi devices.
## Testing
- Existing nscsi target user unchanged by inspection (the new path requires an
  empty control queue, which synchronous targets cannot produce).
- Exercised end-to-end by the SA1403D + Xerox 820-II/16-8 SASI machines in the
  stacked PRs: full boot / CP/M-86 chains, including floppy-over-SASI reads,
  writes and formats (test matrix in the driver PR).

15484: bus/nscsi/sa1403d: Shugart SA1403D rigid/floppy SASI controller [davidlrand]
**Stacked on the nscsi_hle deferred-phase PR** (#15483) —
its two commits appear here until that one merges.
Successor to the withdrawn scsihle-based attempt (#15456).
## Summary
Behavioral model of the SA1400-series microprogrammed controller at the SASI
boundary, per the December 1980 Shugart OEM manual (39022-0):
- up to four drives, any combination of SA1000-family rigid (CHD-backed) and
  SA800/SA850/SA400/SA450 floppy drives as LUN 0-3, selected by DIP switch
  (board location 2H) or fixed by the owning machine via `set_drive_type()`
- class 0 commands (Test Drive Ready, Recalibrate, Request Syndrome, Request
  Sense, Format Drive / Track / Bad Track, Check Track Format, Read, Write,
  Seek), class 1 Copy Blocks, class 6 Define Floppy Disk Track Format with the
  per-OEM-firmware format codes
- completion status `LUN<<5 | error<<1` + 00 message; 4-byte sense; logical
  address `(cyl * heads + head) * spt + (sector - 1)` — all per the manual
The floppy section goes through an internal wd_fdc standing in for the board's
microcode-driven serdes/data separator, at the flux level: **writes and
formats are as real as reads**, and wrong-density accesses fail with the
documented ID-address-mark errors (the Xerox 820-II boot ROM's density
probing depends on this). Track formats are the manual's figure 9 (FM IBM
3740 / MFM IBM System 34, cylinder 0 side 0 always FM in double density).
Slow medium operations return from `scsi_command()` with nothing queued and
complete via the deferred phase transitions from the stacked nscsi_hle change.
Two documented deviations, both invisible at the SASI boundary to known
consumers: transfers stage through a whole-transfer buffer (inter-sector REQ
cadence differs from the single-sector buffer), and READ/WRITE on unformatted
media returns record-not-found instead of hanging until RST.
## References
- Shugart SA1403D OEM manual 39022-0 (Dec 1980).
- The recovered Xerox 820-II/16-8 SASI driver source (Balcones v5.0, dev disk
  B23D13), validated against:
  https://github.com/davidlrand/mame-system-media (Xerox-820-16-8/source/rom-v50)
## Testing
One-liners; full matrix in the stacked xerox820 driver PR:
- `x820iis` (820-II SASI): boots CP/M 2.2 from 8" floppy behind the SA1403D;
  rigid partitions E-H format, SYSGEN and `LG` rigid-boot.
- `x168s` / `x1685s` (16/8): boot CP/M-80 from the rigid disk (`LE`), then
  LOAD86 / 86CON to CP/M-86 1.1F; DIR/STAT on the rigid partition.
- Floppy-over-SASI write path: `PIP E:=A:<file>` runs inside emulation were
  used to build the published rigid CHD.
## Follow-up
After the stacked driver PR converts x820iis off `bus/scsi/sa1403d`, that
legacy skeleton has no remaining users and can be retired in a follow-up.

15485: xerox/xerox820.cpp: 820-II disk personalities, SASI rigid disks, and the 16/8 8086 coprocessor [davidlrand]
**Stacked on:** the SA1403D PR (#15484, which carries the
nscsi_hle PR), **#15461** (bigboard-525) and **#15467** (v50v018 u35) — all
merged into this branch; their commits show in the diff until they land.
**Supersedes** the now-closed #15457.
## Summary
Makes the whole 820-II/16-8 line boot from real recovered media: the 820-II
in all four factory disk personalities, and the 16/8 all the way to CP/M-86
1.1F on the 8086 board.
### 820-II fixes
- FD1797 data bus is not inverted (only the first-gen FD1771's is); the
  inverted not-ready bit made the boot ROM misread a ready drive.
- Density select: port 0x30 = FM, 0x31 = MFM (was reversed).
- FD1797 SSO is unconnected on the main board (side select is a system latch
  bit); SSO no longer drives the connectors.
- FDC /NMI gated on Z80 /HALT, evaluated on both the DRQ/INTRQ and HALT edges
  (a DRQ asserting just before the CPU reaches HALT was lost — hang).
- x820kb: emit the 8748's power-on 0x9E announce that opens the monitor ISR's
  input gate; strobe KBSTB with hardware polarity/order (low = byte valid,
  rising = IRQ). The inverted mapping delivered every keystroke one byte late.
- FD1797 clock primed from the connected drive's form factor (5.25" drives
  never triggered the rate change off the 8" default).
### SASI conversion
`x820iis` converted from the `scsi_port` skeleton to nscsi: new line-level
9R80758 host-adapter bridge ("u8" Z80PIO: port A = SASI data with
PARDY-clocked ACK, port B = control lines) + the SA1403D serving floppies and
rigid disk. Daughterboard personality / A-E swap reported on PA0-1.
### 16/8 8086 board (now modelled; was "not yet modelled")
- 256K RAM, boot ROM at 0xFF000, Z80 banked window into resident/mailbox RAM.
- Coprocessor stop/start/lock on ports A0/A1; counted Z80->8086 doorbell
  latch with INTA off the floating bus (vector 0xFF) — a held line merges
  back-to-back rings and hangs the 86CON console.
- The mailbox lock protocol's unlocked Z80 RMW vs. the 8086's locked ops:
  the shared-RAM bus arbiter modelled as a load-locked/store-conditional
  reservation measured in Z80 cycles (immune to MAME scheduling).
- Z80 timeslice ends at the 8086 release + fine interleave during POST so
  LOAD86's presence poll cannot read stale RAM.
- RX024 floppy box and rgd5 rigid-disk-unit controller ROMs (undumped)
  reconstructed around the recovered Balcones driver source/binaries (B23D13).
- New `xerox_lpk` device: the Low Profile Keyboard serial protocol.
### New machines
`x820ii5` (5.25"), `x820iis` (SASI), `x820iilp` (LP keyboard), `x1685`
(16/8 5.25"), `x1685s` (16/8 5.25" rigid disk unit), `x168s` (16/8 SASI).
### ROM set updates
v4.00 / v4.01 monitor sets and the v4.04 u36 (RX ver 016, 27-Sep-83,
best-available pair for v4.04) replace NO_DUMPs. These are not chip reads:
they are ROM images recovered from Balcones/Xerox distribution master disks
in the Don Maslin 820-II archive (bitsavers `820ii_images`; B16D35 ROM400.COM
split, B17D7 U3x.HEX/U36.ROM decodes), functionally validated by booting.
Marked as plain dumps with provenance in the comments (master-disk builds
verified by execution, not questionable reads). Images + recovered source:
https://github.com/davidlrand/mame-system-media (Xerox-820-line-roms).

15493: cpu/ns32000: account for bus width in operand access timing [davidlrand]
## Summary
`top()` charged 16-bit-bus operand timings on every NS32000 variant:
the `Width` template parameter was never consulted, so an aligned
32-bit operand cost two bus cycles on an NS32032 -- double the correct
figure -- making 32-bit-bus parts uniformly slow.
The change generalizes `top()` to the device's bus width.  The cycle
structure is the one already implicit in the existing table: first bus
cycle 3 (4 with address translation), each additional one 4, with the
bus-cycle count derived from operand size, alignment, and bus width.
- **Width=1 (NS32016)** reproduces the previous values exactly: no
  behaviour change for any existing 32016 machine.
- **Width=2 (NS32032/32332/32532)** stops double-charging aligned
  32-bit operands.
- **Width=0 (NS32008)** is now correctly charged 8-bit-bus timings
  (it previously received 16-bit values).
## Calibration
Measured against period hardware data: dhrystone 1.1 (LOOPS=50000,
compiled, assembled and linked with the 1985 Green Hills C 1.6.12 /
DSI-32000 AS/LN toolchain running on an emulated Definicon DSI-32,
NS32032-10) and the published dhrystone-list result for the real
board (1282-1315 dhrystones/second):
| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| 50K-pass binary | ~970 d/s | ~1292 d/s |
| 500K-pass binary | ~1100 d/s | ~1362 d/s |
i.e. from 76-86% of real-hardware speed to 100-106%.
Toolchain, benchmark provenance and the measurement harness:
https://github.com/davidlrand/mame-system-media (Definicon-DSI-32;
a driver PR for the DSI-32 ISA card follows separately).

15494: bus/isa: add Definicon DSI-32 coprocessor board [davidlrand]
## Summary
New ISA8 card device: the Definicon Systems DSI-32 coprocessor board
(1985) -- NS32032 at 10 MHz with NS32081 FPU, NS32082 MMU, 2 MB DRAM
and an SCN2681 DUART, documented in a two-part BYTE series
(August/September 1985).  Available on any ISA machine, e.g.
`mame ibm5160 -isa4 "" -isa5 dsi32`.
The board carries no firmware.  The host sees a 64 KB paged window
into the 32032's address space (segment E000h, page latch 160h,
control port 150h; jumper-alternate D000h/2B0h/2A0h) and loads the
bootstrap, the 32IO kernel and the user program through it before
releasing the CPU from reset; a mailbox page at 32032 address 2000h
carries service requests both ways, with the DUART's OP5 output
driving the PC's IRQ2.  The DUART is decoded at both of its board
revisions' locations (200100h and F7FE00h).
Modelled from the original Definicon loader sources and the 32IO
kernel source, preserved with the rest of the software at
https://github.com/davidlrand/mame-system-media (Definicon-DSI-32),
including ready-made PC-DOS and CP/M-86 boot disks.
## Verification
With unmodified production software:
- PC-DOS 2.10 + `LOAD.EXE` (and `LOADINT`, the interrupt-driven
  variant, exercising the IRQ2 path) boots the shipped 32IO kernel;
  the Definicon 32000 Monitor signs on, disassembles live 32IO
  memory, and exits cleanly.
- CP/M-86 1.1 + `LOAD.CMD` likewise.
- The period toolchain self-hosts on the card: Green Hills C 1.6.12
  compiled dhrystone 1.1 on the emulated board byte-identically to an
  independent reference, and the DSI-32000 assembler and linker
  produced the executable.
- That binary scores 1290-1360 dhrystones/second against the
  published 1282-1315 for the real board (with #15493 applied).
I designed the DSI-32's loader and I/O system at Definicon and am on
the byline of the BYTE articles.
Software for the card (the production loader and 32000-side I/O system, the Definicon monitor, the Green Hills toolchains,
  ready-to-run host boot disks, documentation, and the period source archives) is preserved at
  https://github.com/davidlrand/mame-system-media/tree/main/Definicon-DSI-32

15495: apple2e: machine_config consistency [arekkusu42]
(followup #14388)
This commit fixes a few `machine_config` problems:

15497: cpu/i86: support port I/O wait states (IOCHRDY) [davidlrand]
IN/OUT/INS/OUTS (and the 80186/80286 REP forms) restart the bus cycle when a device wait-states it via retry_access(), so ISA
  IOCHRDY can be modelled. Register/SI/DI/CX updates are deferred until the access completes; behavior is unchanged for devices that
  never request a retry.
Developed for and tested with a 74LS646-latched coprocessor board on ibm5170 whose PAL wait-states both directions of host port I/O
  (hardware-paced REP INSW/OUTSW with no software polling); also regression-tested ibm5150/5160/5170 DOS boots.

15498: bus/pc_kbd: correct PC/AT 84-key matrix, add natural-keyboard control chars [davidlrand]
Seven keys sat at matrix positions the 8048 firmware does not scan as those keys, verified empirically: every matrix position
  (including unused ones, temporarily declared) was driven individually and the BIOS-observed scancode/shift-flag/type-ahead result
  recorded. Quote/[/= were one bit off; CapsLock, RShift, ] and backquote were in other rows. With RShift at its working position it
  regains UCHAR_SHIFT_1, fixing all shifted characters in natural-keyboard mode (previously every shifted char typed as its
  unshifted partner). Control codes added as the shift-2 PORT_CHAR entry so ^C etc. work naturally.
Verified on ibm5170 PC-DOS 3.20: a probe line exercising every corrected key round-trips exactly, and Ctrl-C renders ^C and cancels
  the line.

15499: machine/ns32082: fix PTE address composition; bypass interrupt-ack cycles [davidlrand]
Two fixes found bringing up a demand-paged UNIX System V R2 on an NS32016+NS32082 board:
1. translate() composed the physical address from the level-1 PTE's MS bit instead of the level-2 PTE's.
2. Interrupt-acknowledge / end-of-interrupt cycles (ST 4-7) are now passed through untranslated: the ICU sits at a fixed physical
  address that no OS maps, so translating these cycles aborts inside interrupt entry as soon as supervisor translation is on.
Tested: full SVR2 boot with supervisor+user dual-space translation and demand-paged exec; vectored interrupts and RETI under active
  translation.

15500: machine/ns32202: level-tracking pending state; priority-gated acknowledge [davidlrand]
Three related fixes, found running an OS whose spl() implementation uses the ICU exactly as the datasheet allows:
1. Level-triggered IPND bits now track their lines continuously, including when ELTG/TPL writes change a bit's effective sense —
  software dismisses and re-arms latched interrupts by toggling between level and edge sensing.
2. In NTAR mode, acknowledge no longer hands out a pending interrupt that an in-service interrupt blocks under fixed priority
  (equal-priority cascade exempt); a blocked acknowledge returns the spurious vector.
3. The INT output is re-evaluated properly after acknowledge; the previous shortcut left it asserted when only blocked interrupts
  were pending, causing phantom nested interrupts the moment a handler re-enabled them.
Tested with an NS32016 SVR2 kernel that implements software priority levels by planting ISRV bits (never touching IMSK at runtime)
  and dismisses its host doorbell via the ELTG toggle — interrupt storms and a reproducible full-duplex protocol deadlock under
  heavy I/O are gone; clock, software interrupts, nested service and RETI all verified under load.

15501: bus/isa: add PD-32 coprocessor board (NS32016, public-domain, 1986) [davidlrand]
Adds the PD-32, the public-domain NS32016 coprocessor published in Micro Cornucopia #32 (Oct-Nov 1986): NS32016 @ 10 MHz + 32081 FPU
  + 32082 MMU + 32202 ICU, 2 MB DRAM, boot EPROM, and a one-word 74LS646 host latch with hardware wait-states both directions (the
  host side uses the i86 IOCHRDY support merged in #15497).
With the NS32082 (#15499) and NS32202 (#15500) fixes, the ZAIAZ port of AT&T UNIX System V Release 2 boots to INIT: SINGLE USER MODE
  with demand paging, mounts multiple filesystems, and survives sustained full-duplex I/O (recursive directory listings, mkfs,
  multi-megabyte cpio installs) and repeated UNIX-to-DOS-and-back cycles.
Modelled from the released PAL equations, boot ROM source and host driver sources. Disclosure: I am a co-designer of the board.

real mem  = 2097152

15505: sound/swp30: saturate AWM2 interpolation output to prevent wrap clicks [charlesvestal]
### Problem
On the SWP30 (MU100 etc.), loud notes produce an occasional one-sample full-scale "click" — most noticeable on bright, sustained,
  near-full-scale instruments (e.g. GM strings). Quiet notes are unaffected, so it presents as an intermittent tick on some notes
  but not others.
### Root cause
In `swp30_device::streaming_block::step()` the 4-tap cubic interpolation result is stored directly into an `s16`. Cubic
  interpolation overshoots the input range near peaks, so when the source samples approach full scale the interpolated value exceeds
  the s16 range and the store **wraps** (+32767 → −32768), i.e. a single-sample sign inversion = an audible click.
Trace of the raw streamed value across one glitch (climbing smoothly, then wrapping):
```
... 26793 -> 30115 -> 32220 -> -32561 -> 32549 -> 31051 ...
                              ^ wrap here
```
### Fix
Accumulate in `s32` and saturate to the s16 range with `std::clamp`. Real hardware saturates (a wrap would be audibly broken), so
  this also matches hardware behaviour.
### Testing
Played GM patches through the `mu100` driver and recorded with `-wavwrite`, counting impulse (2nd-difference) clicks over the
  rendered audio:
| material            | before | after |
|---------------------|:------:|:-----:|
| 6 sustained notes   |   3    |   0   |
| full GM song        |  15+   |   0   |
| quiet/decay patches |   0    |   0   |
Audio that never overshoots is bit-identical before/after; only the previously-wrapping samples change (now saturated).
Note: this is distinct from #11976 (envelope release abruptness) — a separate interpolation-overflow defect, though both manifest as
  "clicks".

15512: upd71071: refactor based on v5x_dmau_device [devinacker]
I'm currently working on bringing up drivers for the Akai S900 and S950, which use uPD71071 DMA controllers in ways that the
  existing implementation in `machine/upd71071.cpp` isn't really suitable for (i.e. where the DMA controller's memory space isn't
  the same as the main CPU's). Since there is already a better implementation in `v5x_dmau_device`, that is now the basis for the
  uPD71071 implementation instead.
The new version also correctly reflects that there are only 3 bytes of address registers, and that the 32-bit DMA addressing on the
  FM Towns involves external logic, as mentioned in the hardware manual. I tested this with a few floppy and CD titles (including
  Brandish and Cameltry since they were both mentioned in the old device's commit history) and didn't notice any regressions.

15513: machine/ncr5385: complete selection as soon as the target responds [davidlrand]
`scsi_ctrl_changed()` only advanced the state machine on a target `BSY` assertion when `SEL` was no longer asserted — the
  post-selection (bus phase) case. During selection the initiator still asserts `SEL`, so the target's `BSY` response landed in a
  branch that only logged and never advanced the state machine. `SEL_WAIT_BSY` therefore ran only when its `SCSI_SEL_TIMEOUT`
  deadline timer fired, so every selection took the full 250 ms.
This advances `SEL_WAIT_BSY` as soon as the target asserts `BSY` (with `SEL` still asserted), so selection completes when the target
  responds. It is gated on `m_state == SEL_WAIT_BSY`, and `SEL_WAIT_BSY` re-checks `S_BSY` before completing, so nothing else is
  affected — a non-responding target still falls through to the timeout.
Found while bringing up the National Semiconductor ICM-3216, whose Z80 I/O processor polls the SCSI status register with a short
  timeout that the 250 ms per selection tripped. The other `ncr5385` users (`tekigw`, `tek440x`, `vp415`) get the same
  latency/correctness improvement.

15527: es5510.{h,cpp}: When writing data to DOL, round towards zero. [cbrunschen]
A bug report by sjs@sojusrecords.com identified residual noise when playing certain sounds on an SD-1 - specifically, the OMNIVERSE
  sound has a reverberating tail that goes to silence on a real SD-1, but retains a never-ending tail on an emulated SD-1.
They investigated this further and found that this was consistent with a different, undocumented rounding difference when writing
  data to DRAM: whereas the documentation suggests a simple truncation of the internal 24-bit value to 16 bits, which is in effect
  rounding towards negative infinity, changing this to rounding towards zero eliminates the residual noise.
This change is based in part on the patch they sent me.
Further, per the es5510 documentation,
> ## 3.5.3 RAM Data Interface
>
> # Data Input Latch (DIL)
>
> [...] The DIL is 16 bits wide, left justified, so programs accessing
> the DIL will see the least significant 8 bits as 0.
>
> [...]
>
> # Data Output Latch (DOL) and FIFO
>
> [...] Like the DIL, it is also 16 bits wide and is left justified.
Accordingly, change the type of `dil` to `uint16_t` and `dol` to `uint16_[2]`; and move the shifting and rounding aspects to when
  the emulation reads/writes DIL and DOL internally, rather than when they values are read or written to/from RAM / IO. This seems
  like it would be closer to how the chip probably works internally.
