-xerox/bigbord2: Boot CP/M, wire SASI HD, mark working (#15412) [David Rand]
 * xerox/bigbord2: Boot CP/M, wire SASI hard disk, fix warm-boot DRQ race
   - Brings the Big Board II driver from "MACHINE_NOT_WORKING" up to a functional state.  Five inter-related changes, all small and
      local to bigbord2.cpp.
   - 1) 74LS151 DMA-RDY mux: track the selected input dynamically. syslatch2 bits 0-2 select which of eight RDY sources feeds the
      Z80 DMA's /RDY pin; the existing driver only re-evaluated the mux inside syslatch2_w, so per-byte FDC DRQ / SIO WRDY edges
      that happen *after* a syslatch2 write never propagated to the DMA. Result: DMA boot read of the CP/M loader sets WD179x bit 2
      (Lost Data) and the BIOS prints "BIOS error on A: drq".  Fix: store m_dma_sel + an m_cc[8] cache of all eight mux inputs, then
      have fdc_drq_w / sio_wrdya_w / sio_wrdyb_w (and sasi_req_w below) call m_dma->rdy_w() whenever their index matches m_dma_sel.
   - 2) Soft-reset re-init.  The state above (m_dma_sel, m_cc[], DMA RDY level, m_fdc_rq, the NMI line) was left stale across a soft
      reset, so the boot DMA on the second cold-attempt session was non-deterministic.  machine_reset() now restores it to the
      power-on values the cold-boot ctors produce.
   - 3) FDC INTRQ + DRQ -> Z80 /NMI gate (HALT-conditional).  Per the schematic, the WD179x's INTRQ (pin 39) and DRQ are OR'd,
      inverted and AND'd with /HALT to drive /NMI -- so the CBIOS' HALT+INI PIO path moves one byte (or finishes a command) per NMI
      while the CPU is halted, but the gate stays quiet during a DMA transfer (CPU bus-released, not halted).  Implemented via the
      Z80 halt_cb() callback (zero polling latency) + a single m_fdc_rq byte tracking INTRQ in bit 0, DRQ in bit 1, and NMI-asserted
      in bit 7.  This is needed for CBIOS variants that use the HALT+INI path (e.g. cbios.s1) on warm boot's CCP reload; the V1.1
      monitor in bigbrdii.u85 is interrupt-driven and never halts, so the gate stays quiet for it.
   - 4) Race-window mask in fdc_status_r.  MAME's wd_fdc::command_end() clears BUSY without dropping DRQ when LOST_DATA is *not* set
      -- there's a literal "TBD: lost data should probably negate DRQ" comment in the code.  That exposes a transient BUSY=0 + DRQ=1
      state real WD179x DMA hardware doesn't (arbitration grabs the trailing byte before BUSY drops).  Without this mask the V1.1
      CBIOS' DISKOP polling exits its "wait for BUSY=0" loop, masks the returned status with AND $9F, sees bit 1 (DRQ) set, and
      reports "BIOS error on A: drq track N sector M" on every warm boot.  Re-assert BUSY in the status read while the race is open
      so the BIOS keeps polling; the DMA drains the data register within microseconds and the next read returns a clean STS=00. The
      byte stays in the FDC's data register -- the mask only delays when the BIOS sees the command complete, it doesn't fabricate or
      hide data.
   - 5) Xebec S1410 SASI hard disk subsystem.  Adds a small in-driver bigbord2_sasi_host_device that bridges between the BB-II's two
      SASI ports (D8 data / D9 status) and the nscsi bus + s1410 target.  D9 status bits map BSY=0x02, MSG=0x08, C/D=0x20, I/O=0x01
      and (for the DMA-variant ST412 BIOS) REQ=0x80.  SEL and RST come in through outlatch1 (U96, an LS259 addressable latch) Q3 and
      Q2.  SASI REQ is surfaced via req_cb() so it can feed mux input 7 to the DMA RDY; the cbios.s2 INIR variant uses the DMA's
      read-status command to wait for REQ, the DMA-variant reads D9 bit 7 directly.  An optional set_seek_timing() call from
      machine_start() opts the s1410 into a realistic ST-506 cylinder-aware seek model (3 ms track-to-track, 85 ms average, 205 ms
      full-stroke at 3600 RPM, 1:1 interleave -- per the April 1982 ST412 OEM manual) -- depends on the s1410 changes submitted as a
      separate PR.
   - PAL ROM declarations for U23 (PAL16L8) and U34 (PAL10L8) are added as NO_DUMP -- the parts have their security fuse blown, but
      the mem/io decode is already hard-coded from the CUPL equations in the existing roms/bigbord2/BB2_U23_Replacement.txt and U34
      counterpart, so the ROMs are cosmetic for the audit.

-**Seagate ST-506 (5 MB, 153 cyl) via the s1410** using the CYLS:306, HEADS:4, SECS:32, BPS:256 CHD geometry the cbios.s2 INIR
 variant expects (HSTTRK*32 + sector LBA, 256 B/sector per the WINSTUFF DPB).  Phase chain completes cleanly all the way through
 DATA-IN -> STATUS -> MSG-IN -> bus-free; 300 KB sustained read takes ~65 s, matching the user's first-hand experience with the real
 hardware ("painfully slow stepper design").  The Xebec ST-412 (10 MB, 306 cyl) path is identical [David Rand]

-- the only difference is the CHD's cylinder count -- but has not been live-validated on this branch. [David Rand]

 relative to the legacy `bigboard` driver. [David Rand]

      functionally complete: CP/M 2.2 boots, floppy R/W is reliable across cold and warm boots, the Xebec S1410 SASI hard disk is
      wired end-to-end and exchanges full READ / WRITE / STATUS / MSG-IN phase chains, sound plays, the CRT renders, the keyboard
      handles control characters, and SIO channel B is reachable via the standard `-rs232b` slot.

 boot floppy, with subsequent warm-boot CCP reloads and file copies between A: and B: completing cleanly. [David Rand]

 205 ms full-stroke at 3600 RPM per the April 1982 ST412 OEM manual) is engaged via the s1410's optional set_seek_timing() hook from

-sony_news: Add NWU-671CA (NWF-671C) install tapes for NEWS-OS 4.1C [Bitsavers] (#15410) [Brice Onken]

-bus/nscsi/s1410: Add an optional cylinder-aware seek-timing model (#15411) [David Rand]
 * Mirrors the dtc510 approach: introduces an opt-in set_seek_timing(track_us, average_us, full_us, rpm, interleave) API that
    replaces the device's legacy flat 85 ms per-command delay with a power-law model fitted to the published track-to-track,
    average, and full-stroke seek times.  No call from a driver -> behavior is unchanged for the existing s1410 users (victor9k,
    mikromik, x37_sasi, db4105, idpartner_sasi).
 * The model state lives on the device:
   - m_seek_model: enable flag (false until set_seek_timing() is called)
   - m_last_cylinder: head position so subsequent same-cylinder I/O is billed at rotational latency only, not a fresh average seek
   - m_interleave / m_rpm / m_seek_track_us / m_seek_range_us / m_seek_exp: the fitted curve parameters
     * scsi_data_command_delay() then:
   - Same-cylinder I/O (dist == 0): rev_us * interleave / spt
   - Cylinder change: rev_us/2 (avg rotational latency) + track_us + range_us * (dist / ncyl)^exp
     * The exponent is solved from the three calibration points so an average-length seek (~1/3 stroke) costs exactly average_us.
        For the ST-506/ST-412 (track 3 ms, average 85 ms, full-stroke 205 ms per the April 1982 ST412 OEM manual) that gives exp ~=
        0.82, which captures the controller's "burst mode" multi-cylinder behavior -- faster than naive 3 ms/cyl stepping for long
        seeks while still cheap on short ones.
     * The header documents the API with ST-506 and ST-412 invocations side by side.  Both drives use identical set_seek_timing()
        parameters because the controller's burst mode adapts to the CHD's reported cylinder count via image->get_info().cylinders
        -- the 5 MB ST-506 (153 cyl) and the 10 MB ST-412 (306 cyl) are distinguished only by the physical media size, not by the
        timing call.

-dsp563xx/hi08: forward DSP HTX writes to the host (#15369) [Felipe Corrêa da Silva Sanches]
 * htx_w only logged the outgoing word; it did not store it in m_htx or update m_isr. Host-side reads of HRX (offsets 5/6/7)
    therefore always returned zero and the host never saw ISR_RXDF asserted, so any 80C535 firmware polling HSR for a reply from the
    DSP after the bootstrap upload would spin forever.
 * Store the word the DSP placed on HTX into m_htx, raise ISR_RXDF (which in turn asserts host-side HRDF and clears DSP-side HTDE
    through hsr_r's view), and let the host's read of the trigger byte at offset 7 clear ISR_RXDF so the DSP can transmit the next
    word.
 * With this in place virusb boots all the way to its interactive UI: the LCD shows the patch / parameter pages and the panel
    buttons drive the menu.

-bus/nscsi: move the opt-in seek-timing model into nscsi_harddisk_device (#15448) [David Rand]
 * The cylinder-aware seek-timing model added to the Xebec S1410 and proposed for the DTC-510 was identical between the two
    controllers.  Hoist it into their common base, nscsi_harddisk_device, so it is written once and is available to any hard-disk
    controller built on it without subclassing.
 * set_seek_timing(), the model state, the fitted-exponent math (seek_time()) and a generic scsi_data_command_delay() now live in
    the base, which applies the model to the SCSI READ/WRITE/SEEK opcodes in both their 6-byte and 10-byte forms.  The base default
    remains attotime::zero unless a driver calls set_seek_timing(), so every existing nscsi_harddisk user is unchanged.
 * The legacy flat 85 ms per-command delay deliberately stays in s1410 and dtc510 -- it is a per-controller quirk, not a property of
    all hard disks -- so those two controllers are byte-for-byte unchanged with no model set, and delegate to the base only on the
    model branch.

-bus/scsi/scsihle: deassert REQ between phases; hold BSY in COMMAND (#15453) [David Rand]
 * scsi_change_phase() now deasserts REQ on entry, so the initiator sees a clean REQ low->high edge with the new phase's C/D-I/O
    lines settled. Previously the per-byte REQ-deassert timer was cancelled by the next phase's re-assert, leaving REQ continuously
    high across e.g. COMMAND->DATA-IN, so an initiator that samples the control lines on the REQ edge read the stale previous-phase
    C/D/I/O.
 * A selected target also asserts BSY on entry to COMMAND, so a fast SEL deassert racing the sel_timer cannot leave BSY low during
    the connection.

-cpu/powerpc: Bug fixes [R. Belmont, debug assisted by Claude Opus]
 * SPR[MQ] doesn't exist on non-601, PCI Macs gets confused if it's present on 603/604.
 * TLBL: Compute both user and supervisor permissions.  PCI Macs run the 68k emulator in user mode.
 * 603: PCI Macs look for processor version 0x00030001 for 603.  Arcade games don't care.
 * 603: RFI unconditionally clears MSR[TGPR] on models with the TGPR mechanism.  Verified against the 603 User's Manual and QEMU.
 * 603: BAT permission violations are supposed to raise DSI, not TLB miss.  PCI Macs use this to trap and emulate accesses to
    NuBus-style I/O addresses.

-formats/imd_dsk: Add save support (IMD write for flopconvert) (#15385) [David Rand]
 * formats/imd_dsk: Add save support
   - IMD has been read-only in MAME's flopimg pipeline since 2017 — load() parses ImageDisk files, but flux-level images (MFI,
      captured floppies, running emulation state) cannot round-trip back to IMD.  This change implements imd_format::save() so
      flopconvert and any future driver-side "save as IMD" path work in both directions for standard MFM and FM media.
   - The save side decodes the flopimg flux representation per track, detects MFM vs FM and cell-size by scoring four common
      (encoding, cell_size) combinations (MFM @ 1000/2000 ns and FM @ 2000/4000 ns covering 250 and 500 kbps in both modes), then
      emits Dunfield IMD 1.20 records.  Per-track detection handles mixed-density disks (e.g. CP/M boot disks with an FM 128-byte
      boot track on cyl 0 head 0 and MFM data tracks elsewhere) automatically.
   - Specifics:
     * New imd_format::extract_track_rich() walks the MFM/FM bitstream once and produces records carrying physical sector order,
        DAM-deleted flag, and CRC validity for both address and data records.  The shared extract_sectors_from_bitstream_*_pc()
        helpers in flopimg.cpp discard this information; rather than widen them and disturb every caller, this format keeps a local
        extractor.  Flux-noise phantom IDAMs are filtered by requiring addr_crc_ok or data_crc_ok at the push site and by
        deduplicating shared sector IDs to prefer the candidate with the best CRC status.
     * New imd_format::detect_track() probes the four (encoding, cell_size) combinations per track and picks the one with the most
        cleanly- decoded sectors, scored by IDAM CRC.  Re-runs per track so mixed- density disks decode honestly without per-driver
        tuning.
     * New imd_format::save() walks tracks in IMD's canonical order, emits per-track records with the right mode byte, sector
        numbering map, optional cyl/head maps, and per-sector type byte (with the compressed-fill optimisation for sectors whose
        bytes are all the same value).  Deleted DAM and bad-CRC sectors round-trip to IMD types 3-8 as appropriate.
     * Header timestamp + comment are preserved across an IMD->IMD pass via a mutable string member captured on load(); other source
        formats produce a fresh "IMD 1.20: <localtime>" header with a short "Created by MAME flopconvert" annotation.
   - Validation:
     * 24 / 24 disks in a local archive of T-200 5.25" DSDD images (mixed-density boot tracks; bad-CRC sectors; rescue-merged tracks
        combined from multiple Greaseweazle captures) round-trip byte-identical at the sector-data level (header timestamps differ
        as expected).
     * Cross-validated against a Greaseweazle-produced IMD of an 8" T-250 bios.001-1.imd disk: byte-identical sectors after IMD ->
        MFI -> IMD.
   - Limitations (warn / refuse rather than corrupt):
     * Refuses to save tracks with mixed sector sizes within a single track — IMD has no way to represent that.
     * Probes the four common (encoding, cell_size) pairs; tracks at 300 kbps (IMD modes 1 / 4) fall back to mode 5 (MFM 250).
        Adequate for all CP/M and PC media; rare exotic disks would need the probe list extended.
 * formats/imd_dsk: Drop mutable comment cache; always emit fresh header
   - Per review feedback on PR #15385: the format manager is a const singleton, so caching per-image state on it via `mutable` is
      wrong (it would race under concurrent loads).  Remove `m_loaded_comment` and the two read sites (clear in load() entry, assign
      at load() exit, preferred branch in save()).
   - save() now always emits the generic "IMD 1.20: <localtime>" / "Created by MAME flopconvert" header — IMD->IMD passthrough no
      longer carries the source disk's first-line text forward.  Header is purely informational; data-emission path is unchanged,
      and round-trip byte fidelity at the sector level is unaffected.
 * formats/imd_dsk: Use std::localtime for portability
   - CI Windows builds (clang-x64, clang-arm64, gcc-x64) failed because localtime_r is POSIX-only.  Switch to dereferencing
      std::localtime's thread-local result, matching the existing pattern in src/lib/util/timeconv.cpp (which uses *std::gmtime in
      the same way). Portable across Linux, macOS, and Windows.
   - Single call from a single-shot save() routine, so the thread-local buffer's reuse hazard does not apply here.
 * formats/imd_dsk: Fix 8" media handling on load and save
   - Two related fixes for 8" floppy disks that surface together because both stem from load() not propagating media metadata:
   - 1) detect_track() probed only 300 RPM cell rates (1000/2000/2000/4000 ns).  MAME's internal flux representation is canonical
      200,000,000 ns/revolution regardless of disk RPM, so 360 RPM media stores its cells at different internal cell_sizes than 300
      RPM media:
   - 300 RPM, MFM 500 kbps: 1000 ns/cell  (5.25" HD, 3.5" HD) 360 RPM, MFM 500 kbps: 1200 ns/cell  (8" DSDD/SSDD) ...
   - The 20% cell-size mismatch overruns the PLL's ~25% lock tolerance for MFM density, so 8" disks loaded through any FF_8 driver
      and saved back to IMD lose every MFM track -- detect_track returns "mode 3 with no sectors" and save emits empty 5-byte stubs.
      Adds the four missing 360 RPM probes alongside the existing 300 RPM ones.  5.25"/3.5" media at 300 RPM is unaffected: the new
      probes simply score zero on it and lose the per-track contest.
   - 2) load() did not call image.set_form_variant() on the loaded image. The floppy_image therefore kept FF_UNKNOWN/variant=0,
      which propagates to any subsequent save (the MFI header records form_factor=0 instead of "8   "/"SSDD") and to MAME runtime
      code that consults image.get_form_factor() for rotation rate / cell timing.  Adds inference from track count + density +
      heads:
     * 77-track  -> FF_8 + SSDD/DSDD/SSSD/DSSD by encoding + sides
     * 40-track  -> FF_525 with the same per-encoding/per-sides logic
     * 80+ track -> FF_525 (ambiguous 5.25"/3.5"; 525 is the IMD-era default and the 500/250 kbps probe selection handles either)
   - When the caller supplied a real form_factor, it is preserved and only the variant is inferred.
   - Validation: a 77-track 8" SSDD CP/M boot disk (Big Board II DDBOOT.S1.imd) with an FM 128-byte boot track 0 plus 76 MFM
      1024-byte data tracks round-trips byte-identical at the sector level through imd -> mfi -> imd, and now also round-trips
      through a real MAME session (mount writable, write a file from CP/M, exit) without losing the MFM tracks.

-output/network.cpp: Re-wrote to fix the worst of the implementation bugs. [Vas Crabb]
 * Note that this doesn't change the protocol, it's still fundamentally unsuitable for things with large numbers of frequently
    updated outputs.  It should work OK for a system with cabinet lamps now, but e.g. something with a bitmapped display won't work
    so well.
 * Allow clients to remain connected across multiple sessions if the user hard resets, launches another system from the system
    selection menu, etc.
 * Allow connections over IPv6.
 * Don't unsafely access the running_machine from worker threads.
 * Ensure buffers remain valid until asynchronous writes complete.
 * Only keep a single write per socket outstanding, batch up messages that arrive in quick succession into single writes.
 * Fixed obviously broken parsing of incoming commands, use of unsafe strtok, and assumption that one read completion will
    correspond to one command (commands must be terminated with CR or LF).
 * Shut down in an orderly fashion and wait for I/O to complete.
 * Made object lifecycles clearer, avoid accessing mutable state from multiple threads as much as possible, protect remaining shared
    mutable state with mutexes.
 * Added some error logging.

-esq5505.cpp and more: Cleanup Ensoniq VFX family layout loading. (#15469) [Christian Brunschen]
 * Since the recent output_finder changes, output names are now resolved relative to the device that creates them. When the layouts
    aer loaded by the esqpanel device, but the outputs created by the esqvfd device contained within it, then for the layout to find
    the outputs it must use a qualified name - "vfd:vfd~n~".
 * If we have to use qualified names for some things, we might as well use them throughout the keyboard-specific layouts (vfx,
    vfxsd, sd1, sd132).
 * This allows us to move the loading of the layouts from the esqpanel device (from which ioports can be resolved without a
    qualified name) to the `esq5505` class's method that configure each keyboard - since the qualified names allow us to find not
    only outputs for use by the esqvfd device, but also io ports that are on the esqpanel device.
 * This in turn allows us to get rid of passing a "panel type" in to the esqpanel device so that it would load the correct layout.
 * All in all a lot simpler.
 * Code updated, and layouts regenerated accordingly.

-cpu/ns32000: account for bus width in operand access timing (#15493) [David Rand]
 * top() charged 16-bit-bus operand timings on every device variant: an aligned 32-bit operand cost two bus cycles on an NS32032,
    double the correct figure, making 32-bit-bus parts uniformly slow.  Generalize to the device's bus width: the first bus cycle
    costs 3 (4 with address translation), each additional one 4, with the cycle count derived from operand size, alignment and bus
    width.  The 16-bit case reproduces the previous values exactly; the NS32008 is now correctly charged 8-bit-bus timings.
 * Calibrated against period hardware: dhrystone 1.1 compiled with the 1985 Green Hills/Definicon toolchain, run on an emulated
    DSI-32 coprocessor (NS32032-10), scores 1290-1360 dhrystones/second against the official result of 1282-1315 for the real board
    (previously 970-1100).

-hd44780: fix char size selection in 2-line mode (#15458) [Devin Acker]

-machine/nscsi_hle: allow targets to defer phase transitions (#15483) [David Rand]
 * An nscsi_full_device target previously had to queue its entire phase sequence (data, status, message) before returning from
    scsi_command(), so a command involving slow media work had to complete synchronously.
 * Make TARGET_NEXT_CONTROL hold the connection in the current state when the control queue is empty instead of popping an invalid
    entry, and resume stepping when scsi_data_in()/scsi_data_out()/ scsi_status_complete() push new entries.  A target can now
    return from scsi_command() with nothing queued, run the medium operation off a timer, and queue the data/status phases when it
    finishes.
 * Targets that queue everything up front never reach the new path; no behavior change for existing devices.

-Add bruteforced PLDs to "triviaes4" [Jordi Tuells] (#15492) [ClawGrip]

Pleiads (bootleg, ManilaMatic) [Trol, Hammy, Porchy]

-cpu/i86: support port I/O wait states via access retry (#15497) [David Rand]
 * IN, OUT, INS and OUTS (including the 80186/80286 REP forms) now restart the bus cycle when a device signals a wait state through
    cpu_device::retry_access(), allowing ISA IOCHRDY to be modelled. Register and string-pointer updates are deferred until the
    access completes, so a stalled cycle has no side effects.  No behavior change for devices that never request a retry.

-bus/isa: add Definicon DSI-32 coprocessor board (#15494) [David Rand]
 * NS32032 (10 MHz) + NS32081 FPU + NS32082 MMU + 2MB RAM + SCN2681 DUART on an ISA card (Definicon Systems, 1985; BYTE
    August/September 1985). The board carries no firmware: the host loads the 32IO kernel and user program through a paged 64K
    memory window (segment E000h or D000h, page latch and control port jumper-selectable) and releases the 32032 from reset; the two
    processors then converse through a mailbox page, with the DUART's OP5 driving the PC's IRQ2.
 * Modelled from the original loader sources (32KH.INC, 32K.ASM, IOPROC.ASM, LOAD.C) and 32IO.A32.  Verified with the unmodified
    production software under PC-DOS 2.10 and CP/M-86 1.1: LOAD.EXE, LOAD.CMD and LOADINT (interrupt mode) boot the shipped 32IO and
    Definicon monitor, and the period Green Hills C compiler, assembler and linker self-host on the card.  Documentation, software
    and ready-made boot disks: https://github.com/davidlrand/mame-system-media (Definicon-DSI-32)

-machine/ns32082: fix translated address composition, do not translate interrupt cycles (#15499) [David Rand]
 * The final physical address used the level-1 PTE's MS bit where it must use the level-2 PTE's, corrupting bit 24 of every
    translation whose PTEs differ in MS.
 * Interrupt-acknowledge and end-of-interrupt bus cycles (ST 4-7) address the interrupt controller at a fixed physical address and
    are not translated; translating them faults inside interrupt entry the moment supervisor translation is enabled, since no
    operating system maps the ICU's hardwired address.

-apple2e: machine_config consistency (#15495) [arekkusu42]
 * make -listslots ordering consistent across apple2 family
 * don't redundantly configure W65C02 and dasm_trampoline
 * tk3000, ace2200: don't use A2E swlist on W65C02 machines
 * ace500: does not have internal gameio, followup #14388

-machine/ns32202: track level-triggered pending state, honor in-service priority on acknowledge (#15500) [David Rand]
 * Level-triggered pending bits now continuously reflect the input lines: a bit clears when its line is no longer active, including
    when ELTG or TPL writes change a bit's effective sense.  Software uses this to dismiss and re-arm latched interrupts by toggling
    a line between level and edge sensing.
 * In fixed-priority (NTAR) mode, interrupt acknowledge no longer acknowledges a pending interrupt that an in-service interrupt of
    higher or equal priority blocks (an equal-priority in-service cascade remains acknowledgeable); a blocked acknowledge returns
    the spurious vector, as the hardware would never have asserted INT for it.  The interrupt output is fully re-evaluated after
    every acknowledge instead of using an approximation that left it asserted while only blocked interrupts were pending.

-minitel2: Implement sound output for DTMF tones and beeps (#14692) [Fabio D'Urso]
 * In the minitel2, sound is generated by a speaker connected to the TS7514 modem chip. A subset of its features are implemented in
    this commit:
   - Mode selection, using MCBC and DTMF lines.
   - Shift register for input control data, using the RTS (clock) and PRD (data) lines.
   - Modem register RDTMF, to select what pair of frequencies to generate while in DTMF mode.
   - Modem register RWLO, selecting what to play on the speaker. In particular, monitoring of the transmitted DTMF tones and beep
      generation have been implemented. Monitoring of TX/RX data tones and volume control has NOT been implemented.
   - Modem register RPTF, selecting a filter on the transmitted frequencies. Only the filter on DTMF tones (to select only one of
      the two frequencies in the current pair) has been implemented.
     * This subset of features is enough to play all the sounds generated by the "demov1" ROM and the original ROM (beep on invalid
        keypress, DTMF dialing tones).
     * This commit also simplifies the pre-existing logic to generate a carrier-detect signal when the line control relay is closed.

-machine/ncr5385: complete selection as soon as the target responds (#15513) [David Rand]
 * scsi_ctrl_changed() only advanced the state machine on a target BSY when SEL was no longer asserted, which is the post-selection
    case. During selection the initiator still asserts SEL, so the target's BSY response fell through to a branch that only logged,
    and SEL_WAIT_BSY was not advanced until the full 250 ms selection timeout elapsed -- every selection took 250 ms.
 * Advance SEL_WAIT_BSY when the target asserts BSY while SEL is still asserted, so selection completes when the target responds.
    The timeout path is unaffected: a target that never responds still falls through to the SCSI_SEL_TIMEOUT deadline.
 * Found while bringing up the National Semiconductor ICM-3216, whose Z80 I/O processor polls the SCSI status register with a short
    timeout that the 250 ms per selection tripped.

-nec/pc88_kbd.cpp: hookup pc88va variant [angelosa]

-es5510.{h,cpp}: When writing data to DOL, round towards zero. (#15527) [Christian Brunschen]
 * A bug report by sjs@sojusrecords.com identified residual noise when playing certain sounds on an SD-1 - specifically, the
    OMNIVERSE sound has a reverberating tail that goes to silence on a real SD-1, but retains a never-ending tail on an emulated
    SD-1.
 * They investigated this further and found that this was consistent with a different, undocumented rounding difference when writing
    data to DRAM: whereas the documentation suggests a simple truncation of the internal 24-bit value to 16 bits, which is in effect
    rounding towards negative infinity, changing this to rounding towards zero eliminates the residual noise.
 * This change is based in part on the patch they sent me.
 * Further, per the es5510 documentation,
 * > ## 3.5.3 RAM Data Interface > > # Data Input Latch (DIL) > > [...] The DIL is 16 bits wide, left justified, so programs
    accessing > the DIL will see the least significant 8 bits as 0. > > [...] > > # Data Output Latch (DOL) and FIFO > > [...] Like
    the DIL, it is also 16 bits wide and is left justified.
 * Accordingly, change the type of `dil` to `uint16_t` and `dol` to `uint16_[2]`; and move the shifting and rounding aspects to when
    the emulation reads/writes DIL and DOL internally, rather than when they values are read or written to/from RAM / IO. This seems
    like it would be closer to how the chip probably works internally.

-machine/ncr5380: fix pseudo-DMA initiator stalls and self-reset IRQ (#15552) [David Rand]
 * Three fixes for ncr5380-family controllers driven as CPU pseudo-DMA initiators, found during pc532 (DP8490) bring-up:
   - state_step(): a DMA wait state polling for a REQ/phase change returned no delay, so state_timer() re-armed at zero, freezing
      emulated time so the target could never change the bus.  Poll at the SCSI REQ/ACK period (100 ns) from those wait states
      instead.
   - scsi_ctrl_changed(): a bus phase change ends the DMA transfer and must deassert DRQ.  Leaving it asserted with phase-match
      clear hangs drivers that poll (DRQ && !PHASEMATCH) for end of transfer.
   - icmd_w(): a bus reset the host commands itself via ICR /RST must not raise a self-interrupt.  device_reset() already deasserts
      IRQ, and the nscsi bus does not reflect a device's own /RST back to it, so only an externally-initiated reset interrupts (via
      scsi_ctrl_changed()).

-rallyx: Replace BANG sample with netlist. (#15424) [Ricardo Bittencourt]
 * rallyx: Replace BANG sample with netlist.
   - Remove the sample from rallyx and replace it with a netlist. The circuit was traced from the service manual directly, and only
      one component had to be added: MB3730_SIL. For this opamp, I tried to match as best as possible the datasheet. Output audio is
      very similar to the sample, but not equal, the sample may have some filtering due to the acoustics of the cabinet.
 * rallyx: Reduce noise and use unified amplification.
   - Changed the MB3730 model to expose a parameter GAIN, whose default is the datasheet typical value. On rallyx, decrease the gain
      of the BANG source to reduce the noise floor, and inject the WSG audio using R28/C28, as in the original circuit.

 * Modernization pass: C comments to C++, some constants converted to constexpr. [R. Belmont]
   - AI disclosure: Claude Opus 4.8 found the 601 alignment exception problem.  The fix is 100% organic by RB.

-machine/ncr5380: make ICR-issued SCSI reset interrupt chip-specific (#15571) [David Rand]
 * A host-issued SCSI reset (writing the ICR ASSERT /RST bit) raises an interrupt on the base NCR5380/53C80: "SCSI reset RST is
    similar to a chip reset, but generates an interrupt", and the chip monitors /RST including the one it drives.  The DP8490 EASI
    is the exception -- its ICR-issued reset performs the chip-reset actions, which "[do] not create an interrupt" (DP8490 datasheet
    6.4 -> 6.2), unlike an externally applied reset (6.3), which does.
 * Gate the self-reset interrupt on the device type so the base 53C80 interrupts while the DP8490 does not.

-sega/model1: fix Virtua Fighter collision via TGP copro-RAM port model (#15582) [Francisco José García García]
 * The copro RAM data port (TGP side) had a bug that broke VF's collision pre-filter (op 0x63), causing skipped knockdowns and the
    characteristic "both fighters fall on one hit" double-knockdown.
 * VF stores each vertex to check as 4 bytes (X/Y/Z/radius) and bits 16 & 17 of the address select the stride for the address
    auto-increment.  VF is the only game that sets the stride to anything other than 1 so this is a no-op for the other games.

-cpu/powerpc/ppcdrc.cpp: Fixed little endian mode selection the 601. (#15592) [Rairii]
 * PowerPC 601 uses a bit in HID0 to enable little endian mode.

-imagedev/floppy: derive twosid_r() from media variant (#15418) [David Rand]
 * imagedev/floppy: derive twosid_r() from media variant
   - Read media-sided-ness from the floppy_image variant tag rather than from observed track data via get_actual_geometry().  Drives
      sense single- vs double-sided media physically (e.g. 8" Shugart TS# on pin 30, from the index-hole position) so the answer
      must come from the media itself, not from whether the host happens to have written to head 1.  An unformatted SSSD diskette is
      still single-sided.
   - Before this change, twosid_r() reported TWO-SIDED to a host BIOS for any freshly-mounted unformatted media (heads==0 from
      get_actual_geometry returns false) and could permanently mis-report after a host wrote spurious noise to head 1 of a
      single-sided diskette.
   - No call-site changes needed: all 17 twosid_r() callers and the uPD765 ST3 plumbing already do the right thing once this returns
      correctly.
 * imagedev/floppy: fall back to geometry when variant is untagged
   - twosid_r() switched on the variant tag with default=two-sided, which relied on every format setting the variant.  Formats that
      never call set_variant() leave it 0 and were silently reported two-sided.  Treat variant 0 as 'unknown' and fall back to the
      previous get_actual_geometry() result, so untagged formats keep their old behaviour while tagged media get the deterministic
      answer.

-mc68681: fix transmitter deadlock in tra_complete (#15564) [David Rand]
 * The transmit-complete handler chose the next transmitter state from the TxRDY status bit rather than from whether another byte
    was queued in the THR.  On a race between a CPU THR write and the final bit-time of the byte being shifted out, two cases could
    permanently wedge the transmitter:
   - completion with TxRDY set and a byte buffered took the "else" path, set TxEMT, and dropped the buffered byte;
   - completion with TxRDY clear and nothing buffered did nothing, leaving the transmitter idle with both TxRDY and TxEMT clear.
     * In either case TxRDY never re-asserts and the transmit clock stays stopped, so a byte never drains and a polled transmitter
        spins forever waiting on TxRDY.
     * Decide from the queue instead: load a buffered byte if one is present, otherwise mark the transmitter idle by setting both
        TxRDY (THR empty) and TxEMT (shift register empty).  This is a no-op on the normal path, where tra_callback has already set
        TxRDY by the time completion is reached.

-sinclair/next/specnext_dma.cpp: Forced Search mode to Transfer instead (#15528) [holub]
 * sinclair/next/specnext_dma.cpp: Forced Search mode to Transfer instead
 * added undocumented logging

-lib/formats/scl_dsk.cpp: Added SCL format support (#15567) [holub]

-sound/swp30: saturate AWM2 interpolation output to prevent wrap clicks (#15505) [Charles Vestal]
 * The 4-tap cubic interpolation result in streaming_block::step() was stored straight into an s16. Cubic interpolation overshoots
    the input range near peaks, so when source samples approach full scale the value exceeds the s16 range and the store wraps
    (+32767 -> -32768) -- a one-sample sign inversion that is audible as a click on loud notes (e.g. bright sustained strings),
    while quiet notes are unaffected.
 * Accumulate in s32 and saturate to the s16 range. Hardware saturates, so this also matches the real device.

-es5510.cpp: fix `WRITE_REG{16}` macros. (#15579) [Christian Brunschen]
 * es5510.cpp: fix `WRITE_REG{16}` macros.
   - As identified by sjs@sojusrecords.com, the `WRITE_REG` and `WRITE_REG16` macros were not writing their 'x' parameter to a
      register as intended, but were instead writing the contents of the `value` variable from calling code. Quite frequently this
      happened to be the correct thing to write, which hid the bug.
 * Remove redundant #ifdef section; LOG_EXEC's use of LOGMASKED already handles the compile-time conditional logging.

-i8259: fix OCW3 write behavior (#15580) [Devin Acker]
 * only set/clear RIS when RR=1
 * only set/clear SMM when ESMM=1
 * exit polling mode after one read cycle

-swa: Removed "Arcade" and added manufacturer name to title, corrected year (#15595) [James Charles Taylor]

-homebrew/pc532: wire the AIC6250 pseudo-DMA; boot System V; mark working (#15607) [David Rand]
 * Wire the AIC6250 SCSI controller's BREQ/BACK pseudo-DMA, mirroring the existing DP8490 path: a CPU-RDY-gated doubleword transfer
    through the 0x3800'0000 window, each byte acknowledged with back_w(1).  Add the AIC6250 DMA window, hook breq_cb (resolving the
    prior drq TODO), and a 10 us quantum for the CPU<->DUART/ICU interleave.  Add a dual-boot BIOS (AIC6250/System V + DP8490/NetBSD
    loader).
 * With the disk path working the board boots System V Release 2.0 to login off the AIC6250 SCSI disk; mark the driver working.

-sega/model1: HUD layering, blink palette and wireframe fixes (#15597) [Francisco José García García]
 * sega/model1: tile palette bit15 = intensity/shade bit
   - The Model 1 tile palette top bit (bit15) is an intensity/shade bit, not an unused 'x' as the plain xBGR_555 decode assumes.
      Almost every entry has it set (full brightness); a handful clear it to render dimmed - e.g. the Star Wars Arcade menu backdrop
      pen (palette[0]=0x7777).  Ignoring the bit left those pens too bright (the FIGHTER CONTROL backdrop rendered bright blue
      instead of the arcade's grey).
   - Replace the COL region's plain palette_device::write16 with a handler that halves the channels when bit15 is clear.  0x7777 ->
      (94,111,119), matching the arcade; entries with bit15 set are unchanged.
 * sega/model1: moiré direct poly on transparent pen fades to backdrop
   - A Star Wars Arcade gunner 2nd-viewport overlay is a single moiré (stipple-translucent) direct poly whose flat colour resolves
      to the transparent texture pen (index 0x1000 -> palette 0x8000 -> black).  Painting the pen literally gives a hard black/scene
      checkerboard over the gunner; the arcade alternates backdrop/scene.
   - Treat a moiré direct poly on the transparent pen as a "fade toward backdrop" overlay and paint the backdrop pen (0x400) so the
      stipple reads backdrop/scene/backdrop/scene.  Scoped to push_direct (2D direct polys) so 3D object shadows are untouched.
 * sega/model1: draw direct polys above the HUD tilemaps
   - The Star Wars Arcade FIGHTER CONTROL / cockpit screens put their callouts, pointer lines and viewport tints in direct
      (2D-projected) polys, which the hardware draws on top of the tilemap HUD.  They were rendered interleaved with the 3D objects,
      so the indicators ended up buried under the HUD.
   - Split tgp_render into two passes selected by render_pass: the first walks the display list drawing only the 3D objects
      (skipping the direct blocks), then the HUD tilemaps are composited, then a second pass walks the list again and draws only the
      direct polys, on top of everything.  Display-list state (matrices, viewports, lighting, RAM uploads) is re-applied identically
      in both passes so the direct polys project with the correct viewport.
   - push_direct previously guarded its vertex projection on z > 0 and snapped behind-"camera" vertices to (0,0).
      project_point_direct only offsets x/y by the viewport centre and ignores z, so that guard spawned huge spurious triangles
      radiating from the screen origin - harmless while buried under the HUD, but a bogus red cross over the SWA cockpit once the
      direct polys draw on top.  Project every direct vertex unconditionally instead.
 * sega/model1: tile colour mode field (blink, unlit UI elements)
   - Bits [11:10] of the tile colour word select a colour mode.  Bit 10 marks a flat UI element (radar blips, HUD text) that is
      drawn unlit at full intensity instead of being shaded by the scene lighting; mode 01 additionally blinks, rotating the BGR
      channels on alternate frames (the radar blips).
 * sega/model1: rasterize degenerate quads as wireframe lines
   - Line primitives reach the rasterizer as a quad with two coincident vertex pairs. Drawing them with the scanline filler
      collapses near-horizontal wires to a few pixels, so detect that case and draw a proper line instead (e.g. the Star Wars Arcade
      target box).
 * sega/model1: draw 0x41 objects above the HUD with a stencil
   - Reorganise rendering into a below-HUD pass (0x01 objects and 0x02 direct polys, sharing the per-viewport z-sort) and an
      above-HUD pass for the 0x41 objects such as the Star Wars Arcade radar blips. The hardware only lets the above-HUD objects
      show through "background" HUD pixels, so the HUD is snapshotted before the pass and its bright features restored over the
      result.
 * sega/model1: invert moiré checkerboard parity
   - Draw the moiré dither on the opposite pixels in both the scanline filler and the wireframe line, matching the hardware pattern.
 * sega/model1: iterate overlay stencil by scanline within cliprect
   - Walk the snapshot/restore buffers with row pointers over the cliprect instead of a flat loop with a divide and modulo per
      pixel, and name the near-black threshold.

-mc68681: assert TxRDY at the THR->shift transfer (#15613) [David Rand]
 * GitHub PR #15564 cured a transmitter deadlock but asserted TxRDY (together with TxEMT) only in tra_complete's idle branch, i.e.
    at end-of-transmission. Per the SCN2681/MC68681 datasheet TxRDY is asserted at the THR->shift-register transfer, one full
    character time earlier, so firmware that pipelines on TxRDY (the Ensoniq VFX inter-board link and MIDI, e.g. sd132) saw the
    ready window a character time late and dropped bytes, tripping SYSTEM ERROR 144.
 * Assert TxRDY in the reload branch where the queued THR byte transfers into the shift register; the idle branch sets TxEMT only.
    This keeps #15564's deadlock cure (TxRDY still re-asserts whenever a byte is queued) while restoring the correct one-character
    buffering window.
 * Tested on sd132 (VFX) and pc532 System V (4x SCN2681 console).

-nec/pc6001.cpp: use a snapshot image for cas and p6 handling [angelosa]

-cpu/powerpc: Updates [R. Belmont]
 * When pages are invalidated with ICBI, only invalidate generated code on that page.
 * For invalidate-everything cases, use a generation counter so that used blocks will recompile on their next call but unused ones
    won't (idea from OG's sound system).
 * STRICT_VERIFY now does a byte-exact position-exact check.  Some Mac OS self-modifying blocks were eluding the simple checksum.
 * New option PPCDRC_STRICT_601_SELF_MODIFY enables monitoring writes to see if they hit a generated code block.  Mac OS Sound
    Manager self-modifies without issuing ICBI (which is valid on 601 hardware due to the unified cache).  This is a significant
    performance hit which is why it's optional.
 * DRC code cache size bumped from 32 MiB to 128 MiB.  The working set of Mac OS 9.1 idling in Finder was over 32 MiB so the cache
    was constantly thrashing.
   - This greatly improves the stability of pmac6100 and makes Mac OS 9.1 fully usable (see my video of it running MacMAME full
      speed with sound from a network share).  Regression tested against an assortment of Konami and Taito PowerPC-based arcade
      games.
   - AI disclosure: Bug finding assistance by Claude Opus 4.8 and it suggested some design elements for the 601 self-modify fix.
      Code is by me.

-bus/xerox820: Restored card-interface device_finder instantiations. (#15641) [David Rand]
 * The copro and disk-daughterboard card device types are exposed through their abstract interfaces via DEFINE_DEVICE_TYPE_PRIVATE,

-sega/model1: repair 2-bit corruption in 315-5711 TGP copro ROM (Wing War, SWA, NetMerc) (#15649) [Francisco José García García]
 * The 315-5711.bin dump has two single-bit corruptions (spurious bit 1 in the low byte, likely a D1 line fault during ROM capture):
   - PC 0x8B: brif alw #0x59e → #0x59c (op 0x34 dispatch) The handler for the specialised affine inverse begins at 0x59c with setup
      instructions (mov $7,x1; mov $4,d). Jumping to 0x59e skips this setup, leaving x1=0x100 from the previous slot, reading
      data[0x301]=ZERO → division by zero → 270 NaN words leak to FIFO.
   - PC 0x67e: lia #2 → lia #0 (op 0x38 record loop) Op 0x38 performs segment-vs-collision-model tests. The loop at 0x67d-0x685
      calls subroutine 0x686 (returns 0=hit, 1..4=miss) and adds a constant via addd. With lia #2, every result was offset by 2, so
      the V60 host always saw 2..6 and never detected hits (brif zrd). The twin idiom in op 0x18 (same subroutine, same handler
      pattern) confirms lia #0 is the intended value.
     * Both games and MAME itself run the unrepaired dump, so the arcade machine is the oracle. Wing War's machine gun now registers
        damage correctly after this fix.

-xerox820: complete the low-profile (G25) keyboard; recover the v5.0 u36 (#15648) [David Rand]
 * xerox_lpk (the G25 low-profile keyboard):
   - add PORT_CODE to every key station; it was PORT_CHAR-only, so nothing mapped in emulated mode (only with -natural)
   - correct the layout to typewriter-paired US, matching the keytops and the v5.0 u36 decode table (shift-2='@', shift-6='^', ...)
   - add the function keys, numeric keypad and cursor/editing cluster from the LPKYBD.MAC keystation table (94 key stations)
     * xerox820:
   - x168/x1685/x168s ship the X928 ASCII keyboard, not the G25; use the standard 820-II keyboard for them
   - fill the v5.0 u36 (was NO_DUMP) with 537p10831 (cda7f598), recovered from the Balcones v5.0 source disk B23D13 (U36-V18.ROM);
      v50 and v50v018 then hold identical romsets, so drop the redundant v50v018 BIOS

-Revert "ns32000: fetch module static base after PSR restore on RETT/RETI (#15633)" (#15656) [David Rand]
 * This reverts commit dbc43a938114322ef0dc8afd7e2207b0e69b74a4.
 * Module descriptors reside in supervisor space: fetching SB through user translation on a return to user mode reads memory where
    no module table exists, clobbering SB on every interrupt or trap return.  Dual-space NS32000 System V (ICM-3216, PD32, Opus -
    all binaries proven on real hardware) fails at the first interrupt taken by a user process.  The PC-MX2 behavior that motivated
    #15633 was ordinary demand paging, not a user-space fetch requirement; it boots correctly with the restored order.  The NS32532
    pc532 (NetBSD and System V) is unaffected either way.

-machine/ncr5380.cpp: implement target mode (#15657) [David Rand]
 * The target role (the file's standing TODO): in target mode the Target Command Register drives the phase lines and, outside DMA,
    REQ (SP-1051 6.4); Start DMA Send sends to the initiator and Start DMA Target Receive is implemented (6.8.1, 6.8.2); a new
    REQ/ACK-handshake state group moves the data.  The Select Enable Register now latches and raises the selection/reselection
    interrupt on a bus ID match (6.6, 8.1), with a guard so a chip selecting outward never matches its own ID.  All target paths are
    gated on MODE_TARGET, which no existing machine sets; initiator behavior is unchanged.
 * First consumer: the ET532 SCSI coprocessor card (NS32532 board operating as a target on the pc532 532SC bus), including
    target-initiated reselection.

-machine/ncr5380.cpp: initiator DMA, data bus and reset fixes (#15658) [David Rand]
 * machine/ncr5380.cpp: implement target mode
   - The target role (the file's standing TODO): in target mode the Target Command Register drives the phase lines and, outside DMA,
      REQ (SP-1051 6.4); Start DMA Send sends to the initiator and Start DMA Target Receive is implemented (6.8.1, 6.8.2); a new
      REQ/ACK-handshake state group moves the data.  The Select Enable Register now latches and raises the selection/reselection
      interrupt on a bus ID match (6.6, 8.1), with a guard so a chip selecting outward never matches its own ID.  All target paths
      are gated on MODE_TARGET, which no existing machine sets; initiator behavior is unchanged.
   - First consumer: the ET532 SCSI coprocessor card (NS32532 board operating as a target on the pc532 532SC bus), including
      target-initiated reselection.
 * machine/ncr5380.cpp: initiator DMA, data bus and reset fixes
   - Four corrections to initiator behavior, each against SP-1051 (the design manual already cited in the header):
     * DMA phase mismatch and DRQ (6.7, 10.3, 10.5.2): a send leaves the request for the next, never-transferred byte pending across
        the mismatch ("the DRQ signal does not reset when a phase mismatch interrupt occurs"); the host completes the transfer by
        cycling DACK once more so ACK can release.  A receive has no pending request, so raising DRQ there fed pseudo-DMA read loops
        a garbage byte.  DRQ is now raised at a mismatch only for OUT transfers.
     * Initiator data-bus release (ICR bit 0, 8.5): the data drivers are enabled only while the phase lines match the target-command
        register; on a mismatch "DB0-DB7, DBP will not be driven even though the ASSERT DATA BUS bit is active".  Previously the
        last data byte stayed wire-OR'd over the target's STATUS byte.  Release-only; the drivers re-enable through the normal
        register paths.
     * Self-initiated bus reset (8.3, 9.3): the base 5380 interrupts on any RST transition including its own ("this interrupt cannot
        be disabled") -- that behavior is unchanged.  The DP8490 EASI diverges: firmware written for the real part (the NetBSD/pc532
        ROM monitor) asserts RST and treats a resulting interrupt as a fault, so the suppression is scoped to dp8490_device only.
     * A DMA wait state that made no progress and requested no delay now re-arms at the SCSI REQ/ACK period instead of zero, so
        emulated time advances and the target can respond (previously hung the emulation).

Validated together: Macintosh Plus/SE boot System 6.0.8 from SCSI to the Finder (with an AppleCD attached); NetBSD 1.5.3 and System
  V on pc532 (DP8490) boot with disk I/O exercise; ET532 target-mode suite.

-devices/machine/wd1002_05.cpp: Support all documented floppy sector sizes (#15665) [David Rand]
 * The WD1015 front-end selects the floppy sector size from SDH bits 6-5 (00=256, 01=512, 10=1024, 11=128) and routes the command to
    the WD2797. The floppy read/write/format paths were fixed at 512 bytes and encoded the WD2797 sector length without the length
    (L) bit, so real Xerox 16/8 media (IBM 128<<N, ID N=2 = 512) read back as 1024 and the Expansion Module II INIT utility could
    not format a disk.
   - derive the selected sector length from SDH bits 6-5 for the host read/write/format byte counts and the buffer bound
   - issue WD2797 read/write with the L bit set (128<<N), matching 16/8 media
   - clear the per-command floppy error before each command: the box ROM's geometry probe issues SEEK-then-READ through a shared
      status poll, so a stale record-not-found must not abort the following command
   - widen the shared WD1002-HD0 board sector buffer to 1K, per the manual

-`esq5505.cpp,{vfx,vfxsd,sd1,sd132}.lay`: add footswitch support and update button documentation. (#15638) [Christian Brunschen]
 * esq5505.cpp: Update button documentation comments.
   - Update names to match labels on the actual keyboards.
     * Adds entries for "buttons" 0 and 1. These correspond to the "Foot Switch" ("Ft. Sw.") jack input for connecting Sustain /
        Sostenuto pedals such as Ensoniq's own SW-1 basic foot Switch or their SW-5 Dual foot switch.
     * Button 29 is "Replace Program".
     * Buttons 30 and 31 are labeled 'Multi A' and 'Multi B' on VFX.
     * Button 52 is labeled "BankSet" on SD-1 and SD-1/32.
   - Also slightly reformat the entries for the buttons that are not present on the VFX, putting their label when present first, and
      documenting the error second.
 * {vfx,vfxsd,sd1,sd132}.lay: Regenerate layouts.
   - Regenerated layouts now include optional foot switches in the Full and Compact views.
   - These are mapped to "buttons" 0 and 1.
   - On the "Master" page, the second line allows assigning `FOOTSWITCH`es, with `FS1` being the left footswitch and `FS2` the right
      one; they can be assigned to sostenuto&sustain, patch selects, and the left (`FS1`) to preset-changing and a few
      sequencer-editing functions.
   - Pedals are hidden by default, but can be enabled in MAME's Video Options menu.
   - Also, buttons now use a single SVG `<image>` with one `<color>` per state, rather than one `<image>` per state.

-osd/sound/coreaudio_sound.cpp: fix a deadlock on exit (#15669) [David Rand]
 * coreaudio_stream::close() took m_stream_mutex before calling AUGraphStop. AUGraphStop blocks until the CoreAudio I/O thread
    finishes any in-flight sink_render callback, and that callback also takes m_stream_mutex -- so holding the mutex across
    AUGraphStop deadlocks: this thread waits for the callback to finish, the callback waits for the mutex.
 * Stop the graph first (which quiesces the I/O thread, so no further callbacks fire), then take the mutex for the rest of the
    teardown.

-comx/comx35.cpp: Consolidate driver in single file [AJR]

-cpu/m68000: Major FPU overhaul [R. Belmont, parts suggested by O. Galibert] This fixes glitched polygons in taitojc.cpp games,
 Marathon now runs properly on 68k Macs, and A/UX gets to a root filesystem panic instead of immediately exploding.  Softfloat 3's
 global state is now saved and restored around execution, so multiple 68k cores can work in a system, or a 68k with an i386. [arbee]
 * FMOVEM.L ctrl-reg predecrement: An decremented by total size first, FPCR/FPSR/FPIAR stored ascending (was reversed; push/pop
    pairs swapped FPCR and FPIAR). Both directions.
 * FMOVE.L #imm,FPCR now actually applies rounding mode/precision (early return skipped the softfloat update).
 * FMOVEM.L #imm with multiple control registers reads one longword per register (was else-if, one only).
 * FMOVEM.X transfer order corrected everywhere: control/postinc FP0→FP7 ascending, predec FP7→FP0 descending (memory image was
    fully reversed; corrupted asymmetric save/restore lists).
 * New apply_fpcr_rounding() called from every FPCR load path; FRESTORE of a null frame resets rounding state.
 * execute_run() saves/restores the process-global softfloat rounding state per timeslice and re-applies FPCR (multi-CPU
    cross-contamination fix).
 * FPU state (FP0-7, FPCR/FPSR/FPIAR, just-reset flag) registered for save states; rounding re-derived on state load.
 * device_reset puts the FPU in documented reset state: NaNs in FP0-7, zeroed control regs, first FSAVE yields a null frame.
 * New GET_EA_FPE() resolves control-mode EAs exactly once per instruction (replaces the (d16,An)-only "offset kludge").
 * FMOVE.X/FMOVEM.X gained (d8,An,Xn) both directions and (xxx).W both directions (were fatalerror), and stores to (xxx).L.
 * Multi-register FMOVEM loads from (xxx).L/(d16,PC)/(d8,PC,Xn) no longer re-fetch extension words per register (read garbage
    addresses and skidded PC).
 * (xxx).W sign-extended in all FPU EA helpers (READ/WRITE_EA_8/16/32/64, fmove_fpcr); missing (xxx).W cases added to
    READ_EA_64/WRITE_EA_16/WRITE_EA_64.
 * Conversion rewritten: float128 and integer based, all 17 digits exact, ±Inf/NaN handled (was host double + string parsing, ~16
    digit limit, no specials).
 * Store k-factor rounding done in exact integer arithmetic with a sticky bit; honors FPCR rounding modes sign-aware (tie case found
    by the IIci ROM's Float_Test).
 * All legal EA modes for packed loads/stores (was a small subset).
 * Dynamic k-factor sign-extends the low 7 bits of Dn (was the raw 32-bit register).
 * k > 17 clamps to 17 digits and sets OPERR/accrued IOP.
 * 4-digit exponents write E3 at the FPSP-documented position.
 * FPU first-word decode moved into m68k_in.lst as 14 per-form entries (fpgen, FScc Dn + per-EA-mode via generator derivations,
    FDBcc, FTRAPcc.W/.L/none, FBcc.W/.L, FSAVE + predec, FRESTORE + postinc); m68040_fpu_op0/op1 nested switches deleted.
 * Fixed dispatch collisions: FScc (xxx).W/(xxx).L misrouted to FTRAPcc on 020/030; FTRAPcc executed as FScc on 040 (wrote a byte
    through (d16,PC)); FDBcc hit the cpdbcc stub (PC desync) on 020/030.
 * FDBcc implemented (decrement/branch, integer-DBcc displacement conventions).
 * FTRAPcc consumes its operand before trapping (taken traps stacked a PC pointing at the operand).
 * Illegal FPU encodings now take F-line exceptions by omission (f27d-f27f, f380-f3ff, FSAVE postinc/PC-relative, FRESTORE predec,
    undefined second-word class) instead of fatalerror.
 * FSAVE/FRESTORE are supervisor-checked (privilege violation in user mode) and follow the PRM-exact legal EA sets, including
    (xxx).W.
 * FSAVE/FRESTORE Frame-pointer off-by-4s fixed: predec/postinc no longer double-adjust; FRESTORE advances match the emitted 28-byte
    (68881 idle) / 4-byte (null, 040 idle) frames exactly.
 * FCMP is a true comparison, not a subtraction: equal infinities compare equal (no spurious NaN/OPERR), NaN operands set NAN only,
    I always cleared per PRM, N mirrors the destination sign on equality (-0 vs +0, -inf vs -inf → N|Z).
 * FMOD/FREM FPSR quotient sign bit is the quotient's sign, sign(dst) XOR sign(src) (was the remainder's sign; SANE reads it for
    trig octants).
 * FPAE accrued-byte constants fixed (OVFL/UNFL were swapped, OPERR collided with DZ); accrued byte is now sticky with the
    documented 68881 mapping.
 * sync_exception_flags maps softfloat invalid → OPERR (non-SNaN); FADD/FMUL/FDIV/FSDIV/FDDIV masks include INEXACT (FMUL also
    OVFL).
 * BSUN (+accrued IOP) set by IEEE-nonaware conditionals on a NaN.
 * FMOVE.S/.D stores clear and sync INEX/OVFL/UNFL uniformly (FPSR no longer depends on destination format).
 * FGETEXP specials: ±0 → ±0, ±inf → NaN+OPERR, NaNs quieted, denormals normalized.
 * FSIN/FCOS/FTAN/FSINCOS reduce arguments ≥ 2^63 by 2π and retry (previously returned the raw argument, |result| > 1).
 * FSINCOS with FPs = FPc keeps the sine result (was cosine).
 * FINT/FINTRZ use extF80_roundToInt in extended precision (were clamped through a 32-bit int, broke |x| ≥ 2^31); both report INEX2.
 * FMOVE.L/.W/.B out-of-range/NaN conversions saturate by source sign and set OPERR, not INEXACT (softfloat's x86-style
    0x80000000-for-positive-overflow corrected).
 * FPIAR is now properly captured by arithmetic-class instructions.
 * FPCR/FPSR writes masked to architecturally writable bits.
 * Debugger FP0-7/FPCR/FPSR registers available on the 68020 (were gated 030+).
 * Stray printf on FTWOTOX removed.
 * std::bit_cast replaces float32/float64 pointer type-puns.
   - AI disclosure: bug analysis, the changelog, the packed decimal encoder and decoder, the comprehensive unit test, and the big
      dispatch refactor were generated by Claude Fable 5.  Additional bug analysis and code review by GPT 5.6 Terra.
   - Test details: A generated unit test Lua boot script (available on request) boots the Mac II to a known state and takes over its
      execution to run code sequences injected into RAM.  In each case the test was run before and after changes were made.  Live
      software testing included dendego, sidebs, and macqd950 running Marathon, Wolfenstein 3D (the Mac port uses the FPU), the
      Jigsaw Puzzle DA (uses FPU trig to generate the puzzle), and the shareware Extended Calculator.

-sega/model1: Clip wireframe lines to the viewport (#15712) [Francisco José García García]
 * The wireframe rasterizer walked its Bresenham from raw projected coordinates. Those come from the unclipped projection, and a
    degenerate projection (z ~ 0 with the boot-time zoom still 0) yields inf/NaN, whose float->int conversion is undefined
    behaviour: x86 hosts get INT32_MIN where arm64 saturates and maps NaN to 0. wingwar generates such vertices while booting, and
    on x86 the resulting (INT32_MIN, INT32_MIN) pairs matched the two-distinct-vertices wireframe case and walked a ~2^31-pixel line
    with overflowing error terms - the gray screen boot lockup reported against GitHub PR #15597 (Windows only, and only when the
    below-HUD pass draws, since the above-HUD pass pushes no geometry there).
 * Before that rasterizer existed these quads went through the scanline filler, which fill_slope()/fill_line() clamp to the
    viewport, so the same garbage stayed harmless and bounded. Give the line rasterizer the same property: Liang-Barsky clip the
    segment to the viewport before walking it. In-view lines are untouched; wires that used to leak a few pixels past the viewport
    edge now stop at it like every other primitive. Attract snapshots of vf, vr, vformula, swa, wingwar and netmerc are
    pixel-identical to master apart from one such leak. Verified against the boot hang by mimicking x86 conversion semantics on
    arm64: hangs before, completes after.

-M68k: MMU fixes and updates [R. Belmont]
 * 68040 form of PTEST fixed and handled (previously didn’t decode correctly)
 * Fixed PFLUSH decoding so incorrect F-line traps don't occur
 * Indirect page descriptors write back U/M bits properly instead of potentially corrupting the page table
 * Reset now properly clears ITTx/DTTx
 * MMUSR now shown in the debugger to help track failed translations
 * PFLUSH on 68881/68030 now properly compare the ATC tag
 * Fixed typo causing misinterpretation of long-format descriptors
 * Indirect descriptors now are handled properly
 * PTEST on 68881/68030 properly handles levels shallower than the table depth
 * PMOVE to TTx now properly flushes the ATC
 * TC consistency check no longer throws exceptions on valid TC values with junk in the TI fields
 * MMU EA decoding now only accepts forms the real MMUs do
 * Added some guards against invalid page tables
   - M68k: FPU fixes and updates [R. Belmont]
 * FPU opcode now validated before doing any EA calculations
 * 68040-specific FSxxx/FDxxx instructions implemented
 * 68881 transcendentals implemented and verified
   - M68k: cycle counting overhauled for 68030 and 68040.  Timings are significantly better but still inaccurate, especially on
      68040. [R. Belmont]
   - 3rdparty/softfloat3: Fixed several errors in the transcendental helpers, including wrong parameter order [R. Belmont]
   - AI disclosure: MMU and FPU debug/tracing and fix planning assisted by Claude Fable 5.  Cycle count refactor assisted by GPT 5.6
      Terra.
   - Testing: A/UX 3.1.1 now boots properly on a range of 68030 and 68040 machines (with some driver changes not included here).  It
      previously was impossible to boot on 68040.  A generated Lua boot script hijacked 68030 and 68040 Macs to run a variety of
      synthetic FPU and MMU tests.  Tested taitojc, Marathon, Wolfenstein 3D, and Jigsaw Puzzle on Mac OS.  Tested NeXTSTEP on 68030
      and 68040.  Tested HP/UX m68k.  Tested funkball on x86 to verify x87 is unhurt by the softfloat3 helper fixes.

-video/zeus2: Fix Cruis'n Exotica road rendering (#15719) [JvM]
 * video/zeus2.cpp: Fix road/near-horizontal surfaces being culled. [mourix]
   - The "Fast HSR Removal" pre-cull tested only the view-space Z of the polygon normal, an orthographic facing test applied before
      the vertex transform. For near-horizontal surfaces such as the road, this sign flips and culls quads that actually face the
      camera, resulting in portions of the Cruis'n Exotica road being missing.
 * video/zeus2.cpp: Add near-plane clipping and perspective-correct texturing. [mourix]
   - Quads straddling the near plane were rejected outright, which dropped the nearest road segment in Cruis'n Exotica. Clip them
      against the near plane instead, as the Zeus 1 renderer does, keeping the visible portion.
   - Textures were U/V interpolated linearly in screen space, which warped the geometry straddling the screen edge. Now carry U/Z,
      V/Z and 1/Z per-vertex and divide per-pixel so texturing is perspective-correct.

-plan80: Driver overhaul [AJR]
 * Correct keyboard, filling in missing key names
 * Use 8255 device for keyboard strobe output and modernize keyboard scanning
 * Add XTAL-based timing parameters for CPU and screen

-xerox/xerox820.cpp: Fixed the 820-II standard-keyboard default (#15710) [David Rand]
 * The standard-keyboard 820-II machines (x820ii, x820ii5, x820iis) defaulted to the v5.0 "RX" monitor, whose firmware decodes
    keyboard input through the u36 position->char table for the low-profile keyboard and does not work with the standard ASCII
    keyboard.  They now default to v4.01, the latest firmware that drives the standard keyboard directly.
 * x820iilp needs its own ROM_START so it can keep v5.0 for its low-profile keyboard (it cannot share x820ii once that defaults to
    v4.01); the 16/8 family already defaults to v5.0.

-m68k: Even more fixes for A/UX [R. Belmont]
 * The 68020+ bf* instructions all had a bug where they could trash the next byte in memory
 * Fixed disassembly of PMOVE
 * Consolidated handling of unimplemented coprocessor instructions
   - apple/djmemc.cpp: A/UX wants the memory controller registers to read back or else it blows up [R. Belmont]
 * A/UX 3.1.1 now boots to multiuser on macqd800, macqd610, and macqd650
   - apple/scsidma.cpp: As theorized long ago, A/UX uses full real DMA mode.  Implemented it. [R. Belmont]
 * A/UX 3.1.1 now boots to multiuser on maciifx
   - apple/maciici.cpp: Hooked up SCSI IRQ and DRQ to VIA2 to try and get m68k Linux unstuck, but no go. [R. Belmont]
   - AI disclosure: Claude Opus 4.8 assisted with the bug hunting.

-debugger/osx/debugview.mm: Fixed a race condition and a QoL issue. [R. Belmont]
 * The Cocoa debugger could push stale scroll position information back to the core.  This manifested most often as the disassembly
    view refusing to update after stepping into a large PC move (JSR/JMP/TRAP or an IRQ gets taken), or the debugger console not
    scrolling automatically.
 * QoL: disassembly views that track curpc (like the one in the main debugger window) should not restore to the saved PC from the
    .CFG file.  Fixes the disassembly view not showing where the real PC is on debugger startup.
   - AI disclosure: the race condition was traced with assistance from Claude Fable 5.

-xerox/xerox820.cpp: mark bigboard5 as working (#15725) [David Rand]
 * The Big Board 5.25" variant boots CP/M from the Xerox 820 software list (the machine already inherits flop_list from xerox820()).
    Verified with the "cpm" entry (130s22100ds_cpmrev3.imd, 40/18/128 FM): the ROM monitor B command loads CP/M, DIR reads the
    directory, and a Ctrl-C warm boot reloads the CCP and returns to the prompt.
 * No 5.25" media was previously present in the tree to exercise it, which is likely why the flag was never revisited.

-sequential/sixtrak.cpp: Improved accuracy: (#15700) [m1macrophage]
 * Corrected the range of the pitch wheel.
 * Added VCF capacitor jitter. Corrected the range of VCO capacitor jitter.
 * Increased the overall audio volume.
 * Added some key bindings to make it possible to play chords with the computer keyboard.
 * Updated the NVRAM, since change first two changes require a tuning and pitch wheel centering.
 * Added a warning in the layout if outdated NVRAM content is detected.
